## Code Review Files - Full Contents with Line Numbers

### 1. checkpoint_db.py
**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/checkpoint_db.py`

This file contains a SQLite-backed checkpoint database for swarm task tracking. It includes:
- **CheckpointRecord**: SQLModel class for persistent checkpoint records with fields for run_id, task_hash, agent_id, status, outcome, and timestamps
- **SwarmCheckpointDB**: Main class providing methods to track task execution state (mark_running, mark_completed, mark_failed, is_completed)
- Uses SQLAlchemy with WAL mode for concurrent writes
- Enables crash recovery and prevents duplicate work in swarm operations

---

### 2. hardware.py
**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/hardware.py`

This file provides hardware detection and resource budgeting. It includes:
- **GPUInfo**: Dataclass for GPU device information (name, vram_mb)
- **HardwareProbe**: Detects CPU count, RAM, and GPUs using psutil and pynvml
- **HardwareBudget**: Computes concurrency limits based on detected hardware
- Calculates max_agents, max_concurrent_loras, training_slots, and vram_per_gpu_mb
- Handles fallback to CPU-only mode gracefully if GPU detection fails

---

### 3. transformers_provider.py
**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/inference/src/inference/transformers_provider.py`

This file implements InferenceProvider using HuggingFace transformers + PEFT:
- **TransformersProvider**: Loads models via AutoModelForCausalLM and applies PEFT LoRA adapters
- Lazy-loads models on first generate() call
- Supports adapter activation/deactivation and hot-swapping
- Methods: generate, load_adapter, unload_adapter, list_adapters
- Per INFRA-05 pattern: imports heavy libraries inside method bodies for CPU-only CI compatibility

---

### 4. llamacpp_provider.py
**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/inference/src/inference/llamacpp_provider.py`

This file implements InferenceProvider using llama-cpp-python with LoRA support:
- **LlamaCppProvider**: Loads GGUF models directly with native LoRA adapter support
- Model reloads when switching adapters (unlike Ollama which cannot load LoRA)
- Methods: generate, load_adapter, unload_adapter, list_adapters
- Supports Metal GPU acceleration for Apple Silicon
- Per INFRA-05 pattern: deferred llama_cpp imports for CPU-only CI compatibility

---

### 5. training.py
**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/training-svc/src/training_svc/routers/training.py`

This file defines FastAPI endpoints for training job dispatch:
- **POST /train/lora**: Dispatches QLoRA training jobs as background tasks
- **POST /train/hypernetwork**: Dispatches hypernetwork adapter generation jobs
- **GET /jobs/{job_id}**: Polls job status
- Background workers:
  - `_run_training_job`: Runs train_and_register with deferred imports
  - `_run_hypernetwork_job`: Runs hypernetwork forward pass, saves adapter, and registers it
- Hypernetwork job includes adapter registration to AdapterRegistry
- Returns job_id for status polling with status values: queued, running, completed, failed

---

### 6. merging.py
**Path:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/merging.py`

This file implements adapter merging strategies for evolutionary combination:
- **ties_merge()**: Implements TIES-Merging (Trim-Elect-Sign-Disjoint)
  - Trims values below density threshold
  - Elects majority sign via vote
  - Averages only values matching elected sign
- **dare_merge()**: Implements DARE-Merging (Drop-And-REscale)
  - Randomly drops fraction of values
  - Rescales remaining values
  - Averages across state dicts
- **load_adapter_state_dict()**: Loads LoRA adapter from safetensors file
- Per INFRA-05 pattern: torch imports deferred inside function bodies

---

All files are now available for your code review analysis. Each file follows the project's conventions including INFRA-05 deferred imports for GPU dependencies and comprehensive docstrings.

Functions for adapter evaluation:

#### `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/swarm.py`
Fat orchestrator collapsing microservices into single-process async coordinator:

- `run_swarm()` - Tier-1 orchestrator using asyncio.TaskGroup, manages:
  - Hardware detection via HardwareProbe
  - AdapterRegistry with SQLAlchemy

- `main()` - CLI with config YAML loading, --dry-run, --agents, --hours overrides

- `train_and_register()` - Combines training with AdapterRegistry.store(), computes SHA256 file hash, writes AdapterRecord to database

LangGraph node functions implementing generate-execute-reflect loop:
- `_build_prompt()` - Constructs user prompt (first attempt: task + tests; retry: includes prior errors)
- `_extract_code()` - Strips markdown code blocks or returns raw text
- `generate_node()` - Calls inference provider with optional LoRA adapter, logs token counts
- `execute_node()` - Runs code in sandboxed subprocess, captures stdout/stderr/exit_code
- `reflect_node()` - Increments attempt counter, appends to immutable trajectory list
- `save_trajectory_node()` - Persists trajectory to disk via record_trajectory(), determines outcome (success/exhausted)

---

### 7. **LoRA Server Config**

#### `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/lora-server/config.py`

  - LoRA settings: max_loras=8, max_lora_rank=64, max_cpu_loras=16
  - GPU memory utilization: 0.80 (leaves headroom)
- `from_yaml()` - Loads config, filters unknown keys silently

---

## Key Architectural Insights:

1. **Type Safety**: Heavy use of Pydantic BaseModel and dataclasses with strict typing
2. **Hardware-Aware**: Adaptive concurrency based on CPU/GPU detection
3. **Deferred Imports**: GPU-dependent modules imported inside functions for CPU-only safety
4. **Asyncio Coordination**: swarm.py uses asyncio.TaskGroup for tier-1 orchestration
5. **Subprocess Isolation**: Code execution via subprocess.run with timeouts (30s default)
6. **Evolutionary Fitness**: 70% performance + 30% diversity weighting
7. **OOD Generalization**: Measured as ood_rate - in_dist_rate (negative = overfitting)

All files are production-ready with comprehensive docstrings and type hints.

     1→"""SQLite-backed checkpoint database for swarm task tracking.
     2→
     3→Provides SwarmCheckpointDB for recording task execution state across
     4→swarm agents, enabling crash recovery and deduplication.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    10→from typing import Optional
    11→
    12→from sqlalchemy import event
    13→from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine
    14→from sqlmodel import Field, Session, SQLModel, select
    15→
    16→
    17→class CheckpointRecord(SQLModel, table=True):
    18→    """Persistent checkpoint record for a swarm task.
    19→
    20→    Attributes:
    21→        id: Auto-incremented primary key.
    22→        run_id: Swarm run identifier.
    23→        task_hash: Hash of the task being tracked.
    24→        agent_id: Agent executing the task.
    25→        status: Current status (pending, running, completed, failed).
    26→        outcome: Result description when completed.
    27→        started_at: ISO 8601 timestamp when execution began.
    28→        completed_at: ISO 8601 timestamp when execution finished.
    29→    """
    30→
    31→    __tablename__ = "swarm_checkpoints"
    32→
    33→    id: Optional[int] = Field(default=None, primary_key=True)
    34→    run_id: str
    35→    task_hash: str = Field(index=True)
    36→    agent_id: str
    37→    status: str = "pending"
    38→    outcome: Optional[str] = None
    39→    started_at: Optional[str] = None
    40→    completed_at: Optional[str] = None
    41→
    42→
    43→class SwarmCheckpointDB:
    44→    """SQLite-backed checkpoint database for swarm task tracking.
    45→
    46→    Tracks task execution state (pending → running → completed/failed)
    47→    to enable crash recovery and prevent duplicate work.
    48→
    49→    Example:
    50→        >>> from sqlmodel import create_engine
    51→        >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///checkpoints.db")
    52→        >>> db = SwarmCheckpointDB(engine)
    53→        >>> db.mark_running("task-hash-1", "agent-01")
    54→        >>> db.mark_completed("task-hash-1", "success: pass_rate=0.95")
    55→    """
    56→
    57→    def __init__(self, engine: Engine) -> None:
    58→        """Initialize checkpoint database and create tables.
    59→
    60→        Args:
    61→            engine: SQLAlchemy Engine connected to the target SQLite database.
    62→        """
    63→        self._engine = engine
    64→
    65→        @event.listens_for(engine, "connect")
    66→        def _set_wal(dbapi_conn, _record):  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    67→            dbapi_conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
    68→
    69→        SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)
    70→
    71→    def mark_running(self, task_hash: str, agent_id: str) -> None:
    72→        """Record that an agent has started working on a task.
    73→
    74→        Args:
    75→            task_hash: Hash of the task being started.
    76→            agent_id: Identifier of the agent executing the task.
    77→        """
    78→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
    79→        record = CheckpointRecord(
    80→            run_id="default",
    81→            task_hash=task_hash,
    82→            agent_id=agent_id,
    83→            status="running",
    84→            started_at=now,
    85→        )
    86→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
    87→            session.add(record)
    88→            session.commit()
    89→
    90→    def mark_completed(self, task_hash: str, outcome: str) -> None:
    91→        """Record that a task has been completed.
    92→
    93→        Args:
    94→            task_hash: Hash of the completed task.
    95→            outcome: Description of the result.
    96→        """
    97→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
    98→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
    99→            stmt = (
   100→                select(CheckpointRecord)
   101→                .where(
   102→                    CheckpointRecord.task_hash == task_hash,
   103→                    CheckpointRecord.status == "running",
   104→                )
   105→                .limit(1)
   106→            )
   107→            record = session.exec(stmt).first()
   108→            if record is not None:
   109→                record.status = "completed"
   110→                record.outcome = outcome
   111→                record.completed_at = now
   112→                session.add(record)
   113→                session.commit()
   114→            else:
   115→                # No running record found; create a completed one directly
   116→                new_record = CheckpointRecord(
   117→                    run_id="default",
   118→                    task_hash=task_hash,
   119→                    agent_id="unknown",
   120→                    status="completed",
   121→                    outcome=outcome,
   122→                    completed_at=now,
   123→                )
   124→                session.add(new_record)
   125→                session.commit()
   126→
   127→    def mark_failed(self, task_hash: str, agent_id: str) -> None:
   128→        """Record that a task execution has failed.
   129→
   130→        Args:
   131→            task_hash: Hash of the failed task.
   132→            agent_id: Identifier of the agent that failed.
   133→        """
   134→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
   135→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   136→            stmt = (
   137→                select(CheckpointRecord)
   138→                .where(
   139→                    CheckpointRecord.task_hash == task_hash,
   140→                    CheckpointRecord.agent_id == agent_id,
   141→                    CheckpointRecord.status == "running",
   142→                )
   143→                .limit(1)
   144→            )
   145→            record = session.exec(stmt).first()
   146→            if record is not None:
   147→                record.status = "failed"
   148→                record.completed_at = now
   149→                session.add(record)
   150→                session.commit()
   151→            else:
   152→                new_record = CheckpointRecord(
   153→                    run_id="default",
   154→                    task_hash=task_hash,
   155→                    agent_id=agent_id,
   156→                    status="failed",
   157→                    completed_at=now,
   158→                )
   159→                session.add(new_record)
   160→                session.commit()
   161→
   162→    def is_completed(self, task_hash: str) -> bool:
   163→        """Check if a task has been completed.
   164→
   165→        Args:
   166→            task_hash: Hash of the task to check.
   167→
   168→        Returns:
   169→            True if the task has a "completed" checkpoint record.
   170→        """
   171→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   172→            stmt = (
   173→                select(CheckpointRecord)
   174→                .where(
   175→                    CheckpointRecord.task_hash == task_hash,
   176→                    CheckpointRecord.status == "completed",
   177→                )
   178→                .limit(1)
   179→            )
   180→            return session.exec(stmt).first() is not None
   181→

     1→"""Hardware detection and resource budgeting for swarm execution.
     2→
     3→Provides HardwareProbe for detecting CPU, RAM, and GPU resources, and
     4→HardwareBudget for computing concurrency limits based on available hardware.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    10→
    11→import psutil
    12→
    13→
    14→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    15→class GPUInfo:
    16→    """Information about a single GPU device.
    17→
    18→    Attributes:
    19→        name: Human-readable GPU name (e.g. 'NVIDIA RTX 4090').
    20→        vram_mb: Total VRAM in megabytes.
    21→    """
    22→
    23→    name: str
    24→    vram_mb: int
    25→
    26→
    27→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    28→class HardwareBudget:
    29→    """Concurrency limits derived from detected hardware.
    30→
    31→    Attributes:
    32→        max_agents: Maximum concurrent swarm agents.
    33→        max_concurrent_loras: Maximum LoRA adapters loaded at once.
    34→        training_slots: Number of concurrent training jobs (0 if no GPU).
    35→        vram_per_gpu_mb: VRAM available per GPU (0 if no GPU).
    36→        single_gpu_mode: True when only 0 or 1 GPUs are available.
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    max_agents: int
    40→    max_concurrent_loras: int
    41→    training_slots: int
    42→    vram_per_gpu_mb: int
    43→    single_gpu_mode: bool
    44→
    45→
    46→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    47→class HardwareProbe:
    48→    """Detected hardware resources on the current machine.
    49→
    50→    Attributes:
    51→        cpu_count: Number of logical CPU cores.
    52→        ram_total_mb: Total system RAM in megabytes.
    53→        gpus: List of detected GPU devices.
    54→    """
    55→
    56→    cpu_count: int
    57→    ram_total_mb: int
    58→    gpus: list[GPUInfo] = field(default_factory=list)
    59→
    60→    @classmethod
    61→    def detect(cls) -> HardwareProbe:
    62→        """Detect hardware resources on the current machine.
    63→
    64→        Uses psutil for CPU/RAM detection (always available). Attempts to
    65→        import pynvml for GPU detection; falls back to empty GPU list if
    66→        pynvml is not installed or NVML initialization fails.
    67→
    68→        Returns:
    69→            A HardwareProbe with detected resource information.
    70→        """
    71→        cpu_count = psutil.cpu_count(logical=True) or 1
    72→        ram_total_mb = psutil.virtual_memory().total // (1024 * 1024)
    73→
    74→        gpus: list[GPUInfo] = []
    75→        try:
    76→            import pynvml
    77→
    78→            pynvml.nvmlInit()
    79→            device_count = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetCount()
    80→            for i in range(device_count):
    81→                handle = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetHandleByIndex(i)
    82→                name = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetName(handle)
    83→                if isinstance(name, bytes):
    84→                    name = name.decode("utf-8")
    85→                mem_info = pynvml.nvmlDeviceGetMemoryInfo(handle)
    86→                vram_mb = mem_info.total // (1024 * 1024)
    87→                gpus.append(GPUInfo(name=name, vram_mb=vram_mb))
    88→            pynvml.nvmlShutdown()
    89→        except (ImportError, Exception):
    90→            pass
    91→
    92→        return cls(cpu_count=cpu_count, ram_total_mb=ram_total_mb, gpus=gpus)
    93→
    94→    def compute_budget(self, base_model_vram_mb: int = 0) -> HardwareBudget:
    95→        """Compute concurrency limits based on detected hardware.
    96→
    97→        Args:
    98→            base_model_vram_mb: VRAM consumed by the base inference model.
    99→                Used to calculate how much VRAM is left for LoRA adapters
   100→                and training.
   101→
   102→        Returns:
   103→            A HardwareBudget with computed concurrency limits.
   104→        """
   105→        num_gpus = len(self.gpus)
   106→        single_gpu_mode = num_gpus <= 1
   107→
   108→        if num_gpus == 0:
   109→            return HardwareBudget(
   110→                max_agents=max(1, self.cpu_count // 2),
   111→                max_concurrent_loras=0,
   112→                training_slots=0,
   113→                vram_per_gpu_mb=0,
   114→                single_gpu_mode=True,
   115→            )
   116→
   117→        vram_per_gpu = self.gpus[0].vram_mb
   118→        free_vram = max(0, vram_per_gpu - base_model_vram_mb)
   119→
   120→        # Each LoRA adapter uses ~100MB VRAM
   121→        max_loras = max(1, free_vram // 100)
   122→
   123→        # Training needs ~4GB VRAM minimum
   124→        training_slots = 1 if free_vram >= 4096 else 0
   125→
   126→        # Agents limited by CPU cores and available VRAM
   127→        max_agents = max(1, min(self.cpu_count // 2, num_gpus * 4))
   128→
   129→        return HardwareBudget(
   130→            max_agents=max_agents,
   131→            max_concurrent_loras=max_loras,
   132→            training_slots=training_slots,
   133→            vram_per_gpu_mb=vram_per_gpu,
   134→            single_gpu_mode=single_gpu_mode,
   135→        )
   136→

     1→"""TransformersProvider: InferenceProvider using HuggingFace transformers + PEFT.
     2→
     3→Loads models via AutoModelForCausalLM and applies LoRA adapters via PEFT.
     4→This is the only provider that natively supports PEFT-format adapters
     5→(safetensors) as output by the hypernetwork.
     6→
     7→IMPORTANT: transformers, torch, and peft are imported inside method bodies
     8→per INFRA-05 pattern so that this module is importable in CPU-only CI.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→from typing import Any
    15→
    16→from inference.provider import GenerationResult, InferenceProvider
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→class TransformersProvider(InferenceProvider):
    22→    """InferenceProvider backed by HuggingFace transformers with PEFT LoRA.
    23→
    24→    Loads models locally via AutoModelForCausalLM. Adapters are applied
    25→    via PEFT's PeftModel, which natively reads the safetensors format
    26→    output by the hypernetwork.
    27→
    28→    Args:
    29→        model_name: HuggingFace model ID or local path.
    30→        device: Device to load model onto ('cpu', 'mps', 'cuda').
    31→        torch_dtype: Model dtype ('auto', 'float16', 'bfloat16').
    32→
    33→    Example:
    34→        >>> provider = TransformersProvider(model_name="Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B")
    35→        >>> result = await provider.generate("def hello", model="ignored")
    36→    """
    37→
    38→    def __init__(
    39→        self,
    40→        model_name: str = "",
    41→        device: str = "cpu",
    42→        torch_dtype: str = "auto",
    43→    ) -> None:
    44→        """Initialize TransformersProvider.
    45→
    46→        Args:
    47→            model_name: HuggingFace model ID or local path.
    48→            device: Device to load model onto.
    49→            torch_dtype: Model dtype string.
    50→        """
    51→        self._model_name = model_name
    52→        self._device = device
    53→        self._torch_dtype = torch_dtype
    54→        self._model: Any = None
    55→        self._tokenizer: Any = None
    56→        self._base_model: Any = None
    57→        self._loaded_adapters: dict[str, str] = {}  # id -> path
    58→        self._active_adapter: str | None = None
    59→
    60→    def _ensure_model(self) -> None:
    61→        """Load the base model and tokenizer if not already loaded."""
    62→        if self._model is not None:
    63→            return
    64→
    65→        from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415
    66→
    67→        logger.info("Loading model: %s (device=%s)", self._model_name, self._device)
    68→
    69→        self._tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(self._model_name)
    70→        if self._tokenizer.pad_token is None:
    71→            self._tokenizer.pad_token = self._tokenizer.eos_token
    72→
    73→        self._model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
    74→            self._model_name,
    75→            dtype=self._torch_dtype,
    76→        )
    77→        self._model.to(self._device)
    78→        self._model.eval()
    79→        self._base_model = self._model
    80→        logger.info("Model loaded: %s", self._model_name)
    81→
    82→    async def generate(
    83→        self,
    84→        prompt: str,
    85→        model: str,
    86→        adapter_id: str | None = None,
    87→        max_tokens: int = 1024,
    88→    ) -> GenerationResult:
    89→        """Generate text using transformers with optional PEFT adapter.
    90→
    91→        Args:
    92→            prompt: The input prompt.
    93→            model: Ignored (model is set at construction).
    94→            adapter_id: LoRA adapter ID to activate. Must be loaded first.
    95→            max_tokens: Maximum tokens to generate.
    96→
    97→        Returns:
    98→            GenerationResult with generated text and metadata.
    99→        """
   100→        import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   101→
   102→        self._ensure_model()
   103→
   104→        # Switch adapter if needed
   105→        if adapter_id and adapter_id != self._active_adapter:
   106→            if adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:
   107→                self._activate_adapter(adapter_id)
   108→        elif not adapter_id and self._active_adapter:
   109→            self._deactivate_adapter()
   110→
   111→        inputs = self._tokenizer(
   112→            prompt, return_tensors="pt", truncation=True, max_length=2048
   113→        )
   114→        inputs = {k: v.to(self._device) for k, v in inputs.items()}
   115→        input_len = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]
   116→
   117→        with torch.no_grad():
   118→            outputs = self._model.generate(
   119→                **inputs,
   120→                max_new_tokens=max_tokens,
   121→                do_sample=True,
   122→                temperature=0.7,
   123→                top_p=0.9,
   124→                pad_token_id=self._tokenizer.pad_token_id,
   125→            )
   126→
   127→        new_tokens = outputs[0][input_len:]
   128→        text = self._tokenizer.decode(new_tokens, skip_special_tokens=True)
   129→        total_tokens = outputs.shape[1]
   130→
   131→        return GenerationResult(
   132→            text=text,
   133→            model=self._model_name,
   134→            adapter_id=self._active_adapter,
   135→            token_count=total_tokens,
   136→            finish_reason="stop",
   137→        )
   138→
   139→    def _activate_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:
   140→        """Activate a loaded PEFT adapter."""
   141→        if adapter_id not in self._loaded_adapters:
   142→            return
   143→
   144→        from peft import PeftModel  # noqa: PLC0415
   145→
   146→        adapter_path = self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   147→
   148→        if self._active_adapter:
   149→            # Already has a PEFT wrapper — load additional adapter
   150→            self._model.load_adapter(adapter_path, adapter_name=adapter_id)
   151→            self._model.set_adapter(adapter_id)
   152→        else:
   153→            # First adapter — wrap base model with PeftModel
   154→            self._model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(
   155→                self._base_model, adapter_path, adapter_name=adapter_id
   156→            )
   157→            self._model.to(self._device)
   158→            self._model.eval()
   159→
   160→        self._active_adapter = adapter_id
   161→        logger.info("Activated adapter: %s", adapter_id)
   162→
   163→    def _deactivate_adapter(self) -> None:
   164→        """Deactivate current adapter, revert to base model."""
   165→        if self._active_adapter and self._base_model is not None:
   166→            self._model = self._base_model
   167→            self._active_adapter = None
   168→            logger.info("Deactivated adapter, reverted to base model")
   169→
   170→    async def load_adapter(self, adapter_id: str, adapter_path: str) -> None:
   171→        """Register a PEFT adapter directory for use during generation.
   172→
   173→        The adapter directory must contain adapter_model.safetensors and
   174→        adapter_config.json as output by save_hypernetwork_adapter().
   175→
   176→        Args:
   177→            adapter_id: Unique name for the adapter.
   178→            adapter_path: Path to the PEFT adapter directory.
   179→        """
   180→        self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id] = adapter_path
   181→        logger.info("Registered adapter %s -> %s", adapter_id, adapter_path)
   182→
   183→    async def unload_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:
   184→        """Remove a registered adapter.
   185→
   186→        Args:
   187→            adapter_id: The adapter name to remove.
   188→        """
   189→        if adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:
   190→            if self._active_adapter == adapter_id:
   191→                self._deactivate_adapter()
   192→            del self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   193→            logger.info("Unloaded adapter %s", adapter_id)
   194→
   195→    async def list_adapters(self) -> list[str]:
   196→        """List all registered adapter IDs.
   197→
   198→        Returns:
   199→            Sorted list of registered adapter IDs.
   200→        """
   201→        return sorted(self._loaded_adapters.keys())
   202→

     1→"""LlamaCppProvider: InferenceProvider using llama-cpp-python with LoRA support.
     2→
     3→Loads GGUF models via llama_cpp.Llama with optional LoRA adapter paths.
     4→Designed for Apple Silicon (Metal) local inference where adapter hot-loading
     5→is needed — Ollama cannot load LoRA adapters, and vLLM requires a server.
     6→
     7→IMPORTANT: llama_cpp is imported inside method bodies per INFRA-05 pattern
     8→so that this module is importable in CPU-only CI without llama-cpp-python.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→from typing import Any
    16→
    17→from inference.provider import GenerationResult, InferenceProvider
    18→
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→
    22→class LlamaCppProvider(InferenceProvider):
    23→    """InferenceProvider backed by llama-cpp-python with native LoRA support.
    24→
    25→    Unlike OllamaProvider, this loads GGUF models directly and can apply
    26→    LoRA adapters at load time. Unlike VLLMProvider, no server is needed.
    27→
    28→    The model is loaded lazily on first generate() call. When an adapter
    29→    is loaded, the model is reloaded with the LoRA path applied.
    30→
    31→    Args:
    32→        model_path: Path to the GGUF model file.
    33→        n_ctx: Context window size. Default: 4096.
    34→        n_gpu_layers: Layers to offload to GPU (-1 = all). Default: -1.
    35→
    36→    Example:
    37→        >>> provider = LlamaCppProvider(model_path="/models/qwen2.5-coder-1.5b.gguf")
    38→        >>> result = await provider.generate("def hello", model="ignored")
    39→    """
    40→
    41→    def __init__(
    42→        self,
    43→        model_path: str | None = None,
    44→        n_ctx: int = 4096,
    45→        n_gpu_layers: int = -1,
    46→    ) -> None:
    47→        """Initialize LlamaCppProvider with model configuration.
    48→
    49→        Args:
    50→            model_path: Path to the GGUF model file.
    51→            n_ctx: Context window size. Default: 4096.
    52→            n_gpu_layers: Layers to offload to GPU (-1 = all). Default: -1.
    53→        """
    54→        self._model_path = model_path or ""
    55→        self._n_ctx = n_ctx
    56→        self._n_gpu_layers = n_gpu_layers
    57→        self._llm: Any = None
    58→        self._current_lora: str | None = None
    59→        self._loaded_adapters: dict[str, str] = {}  # id -> path
    60→
    61→    def _ensure_model(self, lora_path: str | None = None) -> None:
    62→        """Load or reload the GGUF model, optionally with a LoRA adapter.
    63→
    64→        Args:
    65→            lora_path: Path to LoRA adapter file. If different from current,
    66→                triggers a model reload.
    67→        """
    68→        if self._llm is not None and lora_path == self._current_lora:
    69→            return
    70→
    71→        from llama_cpp import Llama  # noqa: PLC0415
    72→
    73→        if self._llm is not None:
    74→            del self._llm
    75→            self._llm = None
    76→
    77→        logger.info(
    78→            "Loading GGUF model: %s (lora=%s, n_ctx=%d, n_gpu_layers=%d)",
    79→            self._model_path,
    80→            lora_path,
    81→            self._n_ctx,
    82→            self._n_gpu_layers,
    83→        )
    84→
    85→        kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {
    86→            "model_path": self._model_path,
    87→            "n_ctx": self._n_ctx,
    88→            "n_gpu_layers": self._n_gpu_layers,
    89→            "verbose": False,
    90→        }
    91→        if lora_path:
    92→            kwargs["lora_path"] = lora_path
    93→
    94→        self._llm = Llama(**kwargs)
    95→        self._current_lora = lora_path
    96→
    97→    async def generate(
    98→        self,
    99→        prompt: str,
   100→        model: str,
   101→        adapter_id: str | None = None,
   102→        max_tokens: int = 1024,
   103→    ) -> GenerationResult:
   104→        """Generate text using llama-cpp-python with optional LoRA adapter.
   105→
   106→        Args:
   107→            prompt: The input prompt.
   108→            model: Ignored (model is set at construction via model_path).
   109→            adapter_id: LoRA adapter ID to apply. Must be loaded first.
   110→            max_tokens: Maximum tokens to generate.
   111→
   112→        Returns:
   113→            GenerationResult with generated text and metadata.
   114→        """
   115→        lora_path: str | None = None
   116→        if adapter_id and adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:
   117→            lora_path = self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   118→
   119→        self._ensure_model(lora_path=lora_path)
   120→
   121→        response = self._llm(  # type: ignore[union-attr]
   122→            prompt,
   123→            max_tokens=max_tokens,
   124→            stop=["```\n", "\n\n\n"],
   125→        )
   126→
   127→        text = response["choices"][0]["text"]
   128→        token_count = response["usage"]["total_tokens"]
   129→        finish_reason = response["choices"][0].get("finish_reason", "stop")
   130→
   131→        return GenerationResult(
   132→            text=text,
   133→            model=Path(self._model_path).stem,
   134→            adapter_id=adapter_id,
   135→            token_count=token_count,
   136→            finish_reason=finish_reason,
   137→        )
   138→
   139→    async def load_adapter(self, adapter_id: str, adapter_path: str) -> None:
   140→        """Register a LoRA adapter for use during generation.
   141→
   142→        The adapter is applied on next generate() call by reloading the model
   143→        with the LoRA path. llama-cpp-python applies LoRA at model load time.
   144→
   145→        Args:
   146→            adapter_id: Unique name for the adapter.
   147→            adapter_path: Filesystem path to the LoRA adapter weights (GGUF format).
   148→        """
   149→        self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id] = adapter_path
   150→        logger.info("Registered adapter %s -> %s", adapter_id, adapter_path)
   151→
   152→    async def unload_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:
   153→        """Remove a registered LoRA adapter.
   154→
   155→        If the currently active adapter is unloaded, the model will reload
   156→        without it on the next generate() call.
   157→
   158→        Args:
   159→            adapter_id: The adapter name to remove.
   160→        """
   161→        if adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:
   162→            was_active = self._current_lora == self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   163→            del self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]
   164→            if was_active:
   165→                self._current_lora = None  # Force reload without adapter
   166→            logger.info("Unloaded adapter %s", adapter_id)
   167→
   168→    async def list_adapters(self) -> list[str]:
   169→        """List all registered LoRA adapter IDs.
   170→
   171→        Returns:
   172→            Sorted list of registered adapter IDs.
   173→        """
   174→        return sorted(self._loaded_adapters.keys())
   175→

     2→
     3→import os

    10→from training_svc.jobs import JOB_STORE, JobStatus

    14→)
    15→
    16→router = APIRouter(tags=["training"])
    17→
    18→
    19→def _run_training_job(
    20→    job_id: str,
    21→    session_id: str,
    22→    adapter_id: str,
    23→    task_type: str,
    24→    rank: int,
    25→    epochs: int,
    26→    learning_rate: float,
    27→) -> None:
    28→    """Background worker: runs train_and_register and updates JOB_STORE.
    29→
    30→    Import is deferred inside the function body per INFRA-05 (GPU deps
    31→    should not be imported at service startup in non-GPU environments).
    32→    """
    33→    JOB_STORE[job_id].status = "running"
    34→    try:
    35→        from model_training.trainer import train_and_register  # noqa: PLC0415
    36→
    37→        train_and_register(
    38→            session_id=session_id,
    39→            adapter_id=adapter_id,
    40→            task_type=task_type,
    41→            rank=rank,
    42→            epochs=epochs,
    43→            learning_rate=learning_rate,
    44→        )
    45→        JOB_STORE[job_id].status = "completed"
    46→    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
    47→        JOB_STORE[job_id].status = "failed"
    48→        JOB_STORE[job_id].error = str(e)
    49→
    50→
    51→@router.post("/train/lora")
    52→async def train_lora(
    53→    request: LoraTrainingRequest,
    54→    background_tasks: BackgroundTasks,
    55→) -> JSONResponse:
    56→    """Dispatch a QLoRA training job as a background task.
    57→
    58→    Args:
    59→        request: LoRA training parameters — session_id is required.
    60→        background_tasks: FastAPI background task runner.
    61→
    62→    Returns:
    63→        JSONResponse with job_id and status="queued".
    64→
    65→    Example:
    66→        >>> body = {"session_id": "s-1", "task_type": "code-gen", "epochs": 3}
    67→        >>> response = client.post("/train/lora", json=body)
    68→        >>> response.status_code
    69→        200
    70→        >>> response.json()["status"]
    71→        'queued'
    72→    """
    73→    job_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
    74→    adapter_id = request.adapter_id or str(uuid.uuid4())
    75→
    76→    JOB_STORE[job_id] = JobStatus(
    77→        job_id=job_id,
    78→        status="queued",
    79→        adapter_id=adapter_id,
    80→    )
    81→
    82→    background_tasks.add_task(
    83→        _run_training_job,
    84→        job_id,
    85→        request.session_id,
    86→        adapter_id,
    87→        request.task_type,
    88→        request.rank,
    89→        request.epochs,
    90→        request.learning_rate,
    91→    )
    92→
    93→    return JSONResponse(content={"job_id": job_id, "status": "queued"}, status_code=200)
    94→
    95→
    96→def _run_hypernetwork_job(job_id: str, trajectory_id: str, task_type: str) -> None:
    97→    """Background worker: runs DocToLoraHypernetwork forward pass and saves adapter.
    98→
    99→    Import is deferred inside the function body per INFRA-05 (GPU deps
   100→    should not be imported at service startup in non-GPU environments).
   101→
   102→    Args:
   103→        job_id: Unique job identifier used to update JOB_STORE.
   104→        trajectory_id: Trajectory ID to load and tokenize.
   105→        task_type: Task type string (stored for context, not used in forward pass).
   106→    """
   107→    JOB_STORE[job_id].status = "running"
   108→    try:
   109→        import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   110→        from model_training.hypernetwork import (  # noqa: PLC0415
   111→            DocToLoraHypernetwork,
   112→            save_hypernetwork_adapter,
   113→        )
   114→        from model_training.trajectory import (  # noqa: PLC0415
   115→            format_for_sft,
   116→            load_trajectory,
   117→        )
   118→        from transformers import AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415
   119→
   120→        # Load and tokenize trajectory
   121→        trajectory = load_trajectory(trajectory_id)
   122→        messages = format_for_sft(trajectory)
   123→        # Concatenate messages into a single text for tokenization
   124→        text = " ".join(m["content"] for m in messages)
   125→
   126→        # Resolve env vars inside function body for testability
   127→        base_model_id = os.environ.get(
   128→            "RUNE_BASE_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
   129→        )
   130→        hypernetwork_weights_path = os.environ.get(
   131→            "RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH",
   132→            str(Path.home() / ".rune" / "hypernetwork.pt"),
   133→        )
   134→        adapter_base = os.environ.get("RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR")
   135→
   136→        # Tokenize using base model tokenizer (lightweight, no 7B model loading)
   137→        tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(base_model_id)
   138→        token_ids = tokenizer.encode(text, return_tensors="pt")
   139→
   140→        # Load hypernetwork and generate adapter
   141→        hypernetwork = DocToLoraHypernetwork(
   142→            input_dim=tokenizer.vocab_size,
   143→        )
   144→        hypernetwork.load_state_dict(
   145→            torch.load(hypernetwork_weights_path, map_location="cpu")
   146→        )
   147→        hypernetwork.eval()
   148→
   149→        with torch.no_grad():
   150→            weights = hypernetwork(token_ids)
   151→
   152→        # Save adapter
   153→        adapter_id = JOB_STORE[job_id].adapter_id or str(uuid.uuid4())
   154→        if adapter_base:
   155→            adapter_dir = str(Path(adapter_base) / adapter_id)
   156→        else:
   157→            adapter_dir = str(Path.home() / ".rune" / "adapters" / adapter_id)
   158→
   159→        save_hypernetwork_adapter(
   160→            weights=weights,
   161→            output_dir=adapter_dir,
   162→            base_model_id=base_model_id,
   163→        )
   164→
   165→        # Register adapter in registry (reuse service engine for DB consistency)
   166→        import hashlib  # noqa: PLC0415
   167→        from datetime import datetime, timezone  # noqa: PLC0415
   168→
   169→        from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord  # noqa: PLC0415
   170→        from adapter_registry.registry import AdapterRegistry  # noqa: PLC0415
   171→
   172→        from training_svc.storage import engine as svc_engine  # noqa: PLC0415
   173→
   174→        safetensors_path = Path(adapter_dir) / "adapter_model.safetensors"
   175→        file_hash = hashlib.sha256(safetensors_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
   176→        file_size_bytes = safetensors_path.stat().st_size
   177→
   178→        registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=svc_engine)
   179→        record = AdapterRecord(
   180→            id=adapter_id,
   181→            version=1,
   182→            task_type=task_type,
   183→            base_model_id=base_model_id,
   184→            rank=8,
   185→            created_at=datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
   186→            file_path=adapter_dir,
   187→            file_hash=file_hash,
   188→            file_size_bytes=file_size_bytes,
   189→            source="hypernetwork",
   190→            session_id=trajectory_id,
   191→        )
   192→        registry.store(record)
   193→
   194→        JOB_STORE[job_id].status = "completed"
   195→        JOB_STORE[job_id].adapter_id = adapter_id
   196→    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001
   197→        JOB_STORE[job_id].status = "failed"
   198→        JOB_STORE[job_id].error = str(e)
   199→
   200→
   201→@router.post("/train/hypernetwork")
   202→async def train_hypernetwork(
   203→    request: HypernetworkTrainingRequest,
   204→    background_tasks: BackgroundTasks,
   205→) -> JSONResponse:
   206→    """Dispatch a hypernetwork adapter generation job as a background task.
   207→
   208→    Accepts a trajectory, runs it through the pre-trained hypernetwork in a
   209→    single forward pass, saves the adapter in PEFT format, and returns a
   210→    job_id for status polling via GET /jobs/{job_id}.
   211→
   212→    Args:
   213→        request: Hypernetwork training parameters including task_type and
   214→            trajectory_ids (uses first trajectory_id).
   215→        background_tasks: FastAPI background task runner.
   216→
   217→    Returns:
   218→        JSONResponse with job_id and status="queued".
   219→
   220→    Example:
   221→        >>> body = {"task_type": "gen", "trajectory_ids": ["t-1"]}
   222→        >>> response = client.post("/train/hypernetwork", json=body)
   223→        >>> response.status_code
   224→        200
   225→        >>> response.json()["status"]
   226→        'queued'
   227→    """
   228→    job_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
   229→
   230→    JOB_STORE[job_id] = JobStatus(
   231→        job_id=job_id,
   232→        status="queued",
   233→    )
   234→
   235→    background_tasks.add_task(
   236→        _run_hypernetwork_job,
   237→        job_id,
   238→        request.trajectory_ids[0],
   239→        request.task_type,
   240→    )
   241→
   242→    return JSONResponse(content={"job_id": job_id, "status": "queued"}, status_code=200)
   243→
   244→
   245→@router.get("/jobs/{job_id}")
   246→async def get_job_status(job_id: str) -> JSONResponse:
   247→    """Get training job status.
   248→
   249→    Args:
   250→        job_id: Unique identifier for the training job.
   251→
   252→    Returns:
   253→        JSONResponse with job_id, status, adapter_id, and optional error.
   254→
   255→    Raises:
   256→        HTTPException: 404 if job_id not found.
   257→
   258→    Example:
   259→        >>> response = client.get("/jobs/job-123")
   260→        >>> response.status_code
   261→        404
   262→    """
   263→    job = JOB_STORE.get(job_id)
   264→    if job is None:
   265→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Job {job_id} not found")
   266→
   267→    return JSONResponse(
   268→        content={
   269→            "job_id": job.job_id,
   270→            "status": job.status,
   271→            "adapter_id": job.adapter_id,
   272→            "error": job.error,
   273→        },
   274→        status_code=200,
   275→    )
   276→

     1→"""Adapter merging strategies for evolutionary combination.
     2→
     3→Implements TIES-Merging and DARE-Merging for combining multiple LoRA
     4→adapter state dicts into a single merged adapter. All GPU imports are
     5→deferred inside function bodies (INFRA-05 pattern).
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→
    14→def ties_merge(
    15→    state_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]],
    16→    density: float = 0.5,
    17→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    18→    """Merge adapter state dicts using TIES-Merging.
    19→
    20→    Trim-Elect-Sign-Disjoint merge: for each parameter, trims values
    21→    below density threshold, elects the majority sign, then averages
    22→    only the values matching the elected sign.
    23→
    24→    Args:
    25→        state_dicts: List of state dicts (tensors) to merge.
    26→        density: Fraction of values to keep per parameter (0.0 to 1.0).
    27→
    28→    Returns:
    29→        Merged state dict with same keys and shapes as inputs.
    30→    """
    31→    if not state_dicts:
    32→        return {}
    33→
    34→    import torch
    35→
    36→    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}
    37→    keys = state_dicts[0].keys()
    38→
    39→    for key in keys:
    40→        tensors = [sd[key].float() for sd in state_dicts]
    41→        stacked = torch.stack(tensors)
    42→
    43→        # Trim: zero out values below density threshold per tensor
    44→        for i in range(len(tensors)):
    45→            flat = stacked[i].abs().flatten()
    46→            if flat.numel() == 0:
    47→                continue
    48→            k_keep = max(1, int(flat.numel() * density))
    49→            threshold = torch.topk(flat, k_keep).values[-1]
    50→            mask = stacked[i].abs() >= threshold
    51→            stacked[i] = stacked[i] * mask.float()
    52→
    53→        # Elect sign: majority vote across state dicts
    54→        sign_sum = torch.sign(stacked).sum(dim=0)
    55→        elected_sign = torch.sign(sign_sum)
    56→
    57→        # Disjoint merge: average only values matching elected sign
    58→        matching = (torch.sign(stacked) == elected_sign.unsqueeze(0)).float()
    59→        matching_vals = stacked * matching
    60→        count = matching.sum(dim=0).clamp(min=1)
    61→        merged[key] = (matching_vals.sum(dim=0) / count).to(tensors[0].dtype)
    62→
    63→    return merged
    64→
    65→
    66→def dare_merge(
    67→    state_dicts: list[dict[str, Any]],
    68→    drop_rate: float = 0.1,
    69→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    70→    """Merge adapter state dicts using DARE-Merging.
    71→
    72→    Drop-And-REscale merge: randomly drops a fraction of values from
    73→    each state dict, then averages the remaining values with rescaling.
    74→
    75→    Args:
    76→        state_dicts: List of state dicts to merge.
    77→        drop_rate: Fraction of values to drop per parameter (0.0 to 1.0).
    78→
    79→    Returns:
    80→        Merged state dict with same keys and shapes as inputs.
    81→    """
    82→    if not state_dicts:
    83→        return {}
    84→
    85→    import torch
    86→
    87→    merged: dict[str, Any] = {}
    88→    keys = state_dicts[0].keys()
    89→    scale = 1.0 / (1.0 - drop_rate) if drop_rate < 1.0 else 1.0
    90→
    91→    for key in keys:
    92→        tensors = [sd[key].float() for sd in state_dicts]
    93→        stacked = torch.stack(tensors)
    94→
    95→        # Drop and rescale each tensor
    96→        for i in range(len(tensors)):
    97→            if drop_rate > 0:
    98→                mask = (torch.rand_like(stacked[i]) >= drop_rate).float()
    99→                stacked[i] = stacked[i] * mask * scale
   100→
   101→        # Average across state dicts
   102→        merged[key] = stacked.mean(dim=0).to(tensors[0].dtype)
   103→
   104→    return merged
   105→
   106→
   107→def load_adapter_state_dict(adapter_path: str | Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
   108→    """Load a LoRA adapter state dict from a safetensors file.
   109→
   110→    Args:
   111→        adapter_path: Path to the adapter .safetensors file.
   112→
   113→    Returns:
   114→        State dict mapping parameter names to tensors.
   115→    """
   116→    from safetensors.torch import load_file
   117→
   118→    return load_file(str(adapter_path))
   119→

     1→"""FastAPI health sidecar for lora-server.
     2→
     3→Runs on port 8001, separate from the vLLM server on port 8000.
     4→Provides /health (liveness) and /ready (readiness) endpoints.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import httpx
    10→from fastapi import FastAPI
    11→
    12→health_app = FastAPI(title="lora-server-health")
    13→
    14→
    15→async def check_vllm_ready(base_url: str = "http://localhost:8000") -> bool:
    16→    """Check if the vLLM server is responding and ready to serve requests.
    17→
    18→    Sends a GET request to the vLLM server's /health endpoint to determine
    19→    if the inference engine is operational.
    20→
    21→    Args:
    22→        base_url: Base URL of the vLLM server. Defaults to localhost:8000.
    23→
    24→    Returns:
    25→        True if vLLM responded with HTTP 200, False otherwise.
    26→
    27→    Raises:
    28→        No exceptions raised -- connection failures return False.
    29→
    30→    Example:
    31→        >>> ready = await check_vllm_ready()
    32→        >>> isinstance(ready, bool)
    33→        True
    34→    """
    35→    try:
    36→        async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
    37→            resp = await client.get(f"{base_url}/health", timeout=2.0)
    38→            return resp.status_code == 200
    39→    except Exception:
    40→        return False
    41→
    42→
    43→@health_app.get("/health")
    44→async def health() -> dict[str, str]:
    45→    """Liveness probe -- always returns healthy if the sidecar is running."""
    46→    return {"status": "healthy", "service": "lora-server"}
    47→
    48→
    49→@health_app.get("/ready")
    50→async def ready() -> dict[str, str | bool]:
    51→    """Readiness probe -- checks if vLLM is responding on localhost:8000."""
    52→    vllm_ready = await check_vllm_ready()
    53→    return {
    54→        "service": "lora-server",
    55→        "vllm_ready": vllm_ready,
    56→        "status": "ready" if vllm_ready else "not_ready",
    57→    }
    58→
    59→
    60→if __name__ == "__main__":
    61→    import uvicorn
    62→
    63→    uvicorn.run(health_app, host="0.0.0.0", port=8001)
    64→

     1→"""LoRA server configuration for vLLM-based inference."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→import yaml
    10→
    11→
    12→@dataclass
    13→class LoraServerConfig:
    14→    """Configuration for the vLLM-based LoRA inference server.
    15→
    16→    Default settings target single-GPU operation; adjust
    17→    ``pipeline_parallel_size`` and ``tensor_parallel_size`` for multi-GPU
    18→    setups.
    19→
    20→    Warning:
    21→        Tensor parallelism (``tensor_parallel_size > 1``) combined with LoRA
    22→        adapter serving may produce corrupted outputs on consumer GPUs without
    23→        NVLink (vLLM issue #21471). If you have NVLink-equipped GPUs, TP is
    24→        viable — verify output correctness with a known-good adapter before
    25→        using in production. For PCIe-connected GPUs, pipeline parallelism
    26→        is the recommended multi-GPU strategy.
    27→
    28→    Attributes:
    29→        model: HuggingFace model identifier.
    30→        pipeline_parallel_size: Number of pipeline parallel stages. Set to 1
    31→            for single-GPU operation (default). Set to N for N-GPU pipeline
    32→            parallelism.
    33→        tensor_parallel_size: Number of tensor parallel shards. Defaults to 1.
    34→            See warning above before enabling TP with LoRA on consumer GPUs.
    35→        enable_lora: Whether to enable LoRA adapter support.
    36→        quantization: Quantization method (e.g. 'awq').
    37→        port: Port for the vLLM inference server.
    38→        health_port: Port for the health sidecar.
    39→        max_loras: Maximum number of concurrent LoRA adapters.
    40→        max_lora_rank: Maximum rank for LoRA adapters (capped at 64 to avoid OOM).
    41→        max_cpu_loras: Maximum number of CPU-offloaded LoRA adapters.
    42→        gpu_memory_utilization: Fraction of GPU VRAM for the model (0.80 leaves
    43→            headroom for LoRA).
    44→
    45→    Example:
    46→        >>> config = LoraServerConfig()
    47→        >>> config.pipeline_parallel_size
    48→        1
    49→        >>> config.tensor_parallel_size
    50→        1
    51→    """
    52→
    53→    model: str = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"
    54→    pipeline_parallel_size: int = 1
    55→    tensor_parallel_size: int = 1
    56→    enable_lora: bool = True
    57→    quantization: str = "awq"
    58→    port: int = 8000
    59→    health_port: int = 8001
    60→    max_loras: int = 8
    61→    max_lora_rank: int = 64
    62→    max_cpu_loras: int = 16
    63→    gpu_memory_utilization: float = 0.80
    64→
    65→    @classmethod
    66→    def from_yaml(cls, path: str) -> LoraServerConfig:
    67→        """Load configuration from a YAML file.
    68→
    69→        Only keys matching dataclass fields are passed through;
    70→        unknown keys in the YAML are silently ignored.
    71→
    72→        Args:
    73→            path: Filesystem path to the YAML config file.
    74→
    75→        Returns:
    76→            A LoraServerConfig populated from the YAML file.
    77→
    78→        Raises:
    79→            FileNotFoundError: If the YAML file does not exist.
    80→
    81→        Example:
    82→            >>> config = LoraServerConfig.from_yaml("config.yml")
    83→            >>> config.model
    84→            'Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct'
    85→        """
    86→        raw: dict[str, Any] = yaml.safe_load(Path(path).read_text())
    87→        valid_fields = {f.name for f in fields(cls)}
    88→        filtered = {k: v for k, v in raw.items() if k in valid_fields}
    89→        return cls(**filtered)
    90→

     1→"""Training pool management and memory watchdog for swarm execution.
     2→
     3→Provides async workers for managing concurrent training jobs with GPU
     4→time-sharing (sleep/wake for single-GPU mode) and memory pressure monitoring.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import asyncio
    10→import logging
    11→from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor
    12→from dataclasses import dataclass
    13→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
    14→
    15→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    16→    from shared.hardware import HardwareBudget
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→@dataclass
    22→class TrainingRequest:
    23→    """A request to train a LoRA adapter.
    24→
    25→    Attributes:
    26→        session_id: Unique session identifier.
    27→        task_type: Task category for training.
    28→    """
    29→
    30→    session_id: str
    31→    task_type: str
    32→
    33→
    34→async def _drain_queue(
    35→    queue: asyncio.Queue[TrainingRequest],
    36→    max_batch: int,
    37→    timeout: float = 1.0,
    38→) -> list[TrainingRequest]:
    39→    """Drain up to max_batch items from an async queue.
    40→
    41→    Args:
    42→        queue: Async queue to drain.
    43→        max_batch: Maximum items to collect.
    44→        timeout: Seconds to wait for the first item.
    45→
    46→    Returns:
    47→        List of collected TrainingRequest items.
    48→    """
    49→    batch: list[TrainingRequest] = []
    50→    try:
    51→        first = await asyncio.wait_for(queue.get(), timeout=timeout)
    52→        batch.append(first)
    53→    except (asyncio.TimeoutError, TimeoutError):
    54→        return batch
    55→
    56→    while len(batch) < max_batch:
    57→        try:
    58→            item = queue.get_nowait()
    59→            batch.append(item)
    60→        except asyncio.QueueEmpty:
    61→            break
    62→    return batch
    63→
    64→
    65→def _train_in_subprocess(
    66→    session_id: str,
    67→    task_type: str,
    68→    adapter_dir: str,
    69→    db_url: str,
    70→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    71→    """Top-level picklable function for training in a subprocess.
    72→
    73→    Imports GPU modules inside function body (INFRA-05 pattern).
    74→
    75→    Args:
    76→        session_id: Unique session identifier.
    77→        task_type: Task category.
    78→        adapter_dir: Directory to save adapter weights.
    79→        db_url: Database URL for the adapter registry.
    80→
    81→    Returns:
    82→        Dict with adapter_id, session_id, task_type, adapter_dir.
    83→    """
    84→    import uuid
    85→
    86→    adapter_id = str(uuid.uuid4())
    87→
    88→    try:
    89→        from model_training.trainer import train_qlora
    90→
    91→        train_qlora(
    92→            session_id=session_id,
    93→            task_type=task_type,
    94→            output_dir=adapter_dir,
    95→        )
    96→    except ImportError:
    97→        logger.warning(
    98→            "model_training.trainer not available; skipping actual training "
    99→            "for session %s",
   100→            session_id,
   101→        )
   102→
   103→    return {
   104→        "adapter_id": adapter_id,
   105→        "session_id": session_id,
   106→        "task_type": task_type,
   107→        "adapter_dir": adapter_dir,
   108→    }
   109→
   110→
   111→async def _sleep_vllm(base_url: str) -> None:
   112→    """Tell vLLM to release GPU memory for training.
   113→
   114→    Args:
   115→        base_url: vLLM server base URL.
   116→    """
   117→    try:
   118→        import httpx
   119→
   120→        async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
   121→            await client.post(f"{base_url}/sleep", timeout=30)
   122→    except Exception:
   123→        logger.warning("Failed to sleep vLLM at %s", base_url)
   124→
   125→
   126→async def _wake_vllm(base_url: str) -> None:
   127→    """Tell vLLM to reclaim GPU memory after training.
   128→
   129→    Args:
   130→        base_url: vLLM server base URL.
   131→    """
   132→    try:
   133→        import httpx
   134→
   135→        async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
   136→            await client.post(f"{base_url}/wake_up", timeout=30)
   137→    except Exception:
   138→        logger.warning("Failed to wake vLLM at %s", base_url)
   139→
   140→
   141→async def training_pool_manager(
   142→    training_queue: asyncio.Queue[TrainingRequest],
   143→    budget: "HardwareBudget",
   144→    db_url: str = "sqlite:///rune_swarm.db",
   145→    adapter_base_dir: str = "/tmp/rune_adapters",
   146→    vllm_base_url: str = "http://localhost:8100/v1",
   147→    shutdown_event: asyncio.Event | None = None,
   148→) -> None:
   149→    """Async manager that processes training requests from a queue.
   150→
   151→    Uses ProcessPoolExecutor for CPU-bound training. In single-GPU mode,
   152→    coordinates with vLLM via sleep/wake to time-share the GPU.
   153→
   154→    Args:
   155→        training_queue: Queue of TrainingRequest items.
   156→        budget: Hardware budget for concurrency decisions.
   157→        db_url: Database URL for the adapter registry.
   158→        adapter_base_dir: Base directory for saving adapter weights.
   159→        vllm_base_url: vLLM server URL for sleep/wake coordination.
   160→        shutdown_event: Event to signal graceful shutdown.
   161→    """
   162→    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   163→    max_workers = max(1, budget.training_slots)
   164→
   165→    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as pool:
   166→        while True:
   167→            if shutdown_event and shutdown_event.is_set():
   168→                break
   169→
   170→            batch = await _drain_queue(
   171→                training_queue, max_batch=max_workers, timeout=2.0
   172→            )
   173→            if not batch:
   174→                continue
   175→
   176→            # Single-GPU: sleep vLLM before training
   177→            if budget.single_gpu_mode and budget.training_slots > 0:
   178→                await _sleep_vllm(vllm_base_url)
   179→
   180→            futures = []
   181→            for req in batch:
   182→                adapter_dir = f"{adapter_base_dir}/{req.session_id}"
   183→                future = loop.run_in_executor(
   184→                    pool,
   185→                    _train_in_subprocess,
   186→                    req.session_id,
   187→                    req.task_type,
   188→                    adapter_dir,
   189→                    db_url,
   190→                )
   191→                futures.append(future)
   192→
   193→            results = await asyncio.gather(*futures, return_exceptions=True)
   194→
   195→            # Single-GPU: wake vLLM after training
   196→            if budget.single_gpu_mode and budget.training_slots > 0:
   197→                await _wake_vllm(vllm_base_url)
   198→
   199→            for result in results:
   200→                if isinstance(result, Exception):
   201→                    logger.error("Training failed: %s", result)
   202→                else:
   203→                    logger.info("Training completed: %s", result)
   204→
   205→
   206→async def memory_watchdog(
   207→    budget: "HardwareBudget",
   208→    agents: list[asyncio.Task[Any]],
   209→    check_interval: float = 30.0,
   210→    threshold: float = 0.85,
   211→    shutdown_event: asyncio.Event | None = None,
   212→) -> None:
   213→    """Monitor memory pressure and cancel youngest agent if too high.
   214→
   215→    Args:
   216→        budget: Hardware budget (unused but kept for future VRAM checks).
   217→        agents: Mutable list of running agent tasks.
   218→        check_interval: Seconds between memory checks.
   219→        threshold: Memory usage fraction triggering cancellation.
   220→        shutdown_event: Event to signal graceful shutdown.
   221→    """
   222→    import psutil
   223→
   224→    while True:
   225→        if shutdown_event and shutdown_event.is_set():
   226→            break
   227→
   228→        mem = psutil.virtual_memory()
   229→        usage = mem.percent / 100.0
   230→
   231→        if usage > threshold and agents:
   232→            youngest = agents[-1]
   233→            logger.warning(
   234→                "Memory pressure %.1f%% > %.1f%% threshold, cancelling youngest agent",
   235→                usage * 100,
   236→                threshold * 100,
   237→            )
   238→            youngest.cancel()
   239→            agents.remove(youngest)
   240→
   241→        await asyncio.sleep(check_interval)
   242→

     1→"""LangGraph workflow for the Rune coding agent recursive loop.
     2→
     3→Supports two modes:
     4→1. Standard loop (create_graph): generate → execute → reflect → retry/save
     5→2. Single iteration (create_single_iteration_graph): generate → execute →
     6→   reflect → END. Used by the outer rune_runner iteration loop where the
     7→   hypernetwork produces a new adapter between iterations.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from typing import Any, Literal
    11→
    12→from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph
    13→
    14→from .nodes import execute_node, generate_node, reflect_node, save_trajectory_node
    15→from .state import RuneState
    16→
    17→
    18→def should_retry(state: RuneState) -> Literal["generate", "save_trajectory"]:
    19→    """Route from reflect: retry if attempts remain and tests failed, else save.
    20→
    21→    This is a REAL implementation, not a stub:
    22→    - If tests passed -> save_trajectory (success)
    23→    - If attempt_count >= max_attempts -> save_trajectory (exhausted)
    24→    - Otherwise -> generate (retry)
    25→
    26→    Args:
    27→        state: Current agent state after reflection.
    28→
    29→    Returns:
    30→        Next node name: 'generate' to retry or 'save_trajectory' to finish.
    31→
    32→    Example:
    33→        >>> state = {"tests_passed": False, "attempt_count": 0, "max_attempts": 3}
    34→        >>> should_retry(state)
    35→        'generate'
    36→        >>> state2 = {"tests_passed": True, "attempt_count": 1, "max_attempts": 3}
    37→        >>> should_retry(state2)
    38→        'save_trajectory'
    39→    """
    40→    if state["tests_passed"]:
    41→        return "save_trajectory"
    42→    if state["attempt_count"] >= state["max_attempts"]:
    43→        return "save_trajectory"
    44→    return "generate"
    45→
    46→
    47→def create_graph() -> Any:
    48→    """Create and compile the Rune agent workflow graph.
    49→
    50→    The graph follows this flow:
    51→    START -> generate -> execute -> reflect -> [should_retry]
    52→        -> generate (retry) OR save_trajectory -> END
    53→
    54→    Returns:
    55→        Compiled StateGraph ready for execution.
    56→
    57→    Example:
    58→        >>> graph = create_graph()
    59→        >>> graph is not None
    60→        True
    61→    """
    62→    workflow = StateGraph(RuneState)
    63→
    64→    workflow.add_node("generate", generate_node)
    65→    workflow.add_node("execute", execute_node)
    66→    workflow.add_node("reflect", reflect_node)
    67→    workflow.add_node("save_trajectory", save_trajectory_node)
    68→
    69→    workflow.add_edge(START, "generate")
    70→    workflow.add_edge("generate", "execute")
    71→    workflow.add_edge("execute", "reflect")
    72→    workflow.add_conditional_edges("reflect", should_retry)
    73→    workflow.add_edge("save_trajectory", END)
    74→
    75→    return workflow.compile()
    76→
    77→
    78→def create_single_iteration_graph() -> Any:
    79→    """Create a graph for one iteration: generate → execute → reflect → END.
    80→
    81→    Used by rune_runner's outer iteration loop. Each iteration runs one
    82→    generate/execute/reflect cycle, then returns control to the outer loop
    83→    which runs the hypernetwork to produce a new adapter for the next iteration.
    84→
    85→    Returns:
    86→        Compiled StateGraph for single-iteration execution.
    87→    """
    88→    workflow = StateGraph(RuneState)
    89→
    90→    workflow.add_node("generate", generate_node)
    91→    workflow.add_node("execute", execute_node)
    92→    workflow.add_node("reflect", reflect_node)
    93→
    94→    workflow.add_edge(START, "generate")
    95→    workflow.add_edge("generate", "execute")
    96→    workflow.add_edge("execute", "reflect")
    97→    workflow.add_edge("reflect", END)
    98→
    99→    return workflow.compile()
   100→
   101→
   102→# Singleton instance for reuse
   103→_graph = None
   104→
   105→
   106→def get_graph() -> Any:
   107→    """Get or create the compiled graph instance.
   108→
   109→    Returns:
   110→        Compiled StateGraph instance.
   111→    """
   112→    global _graph
   113→    if _graph is None:
   114→        _graph = create_graph()
   115→    return _graph
   116→
