     1→"""AdapterRegistry class providing CRUD operations for adapter metadata."""
     2→
     3→import json
     4→
     5→from sqlalchemy import event
     6→from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine
     7→from sqlmodel import Session, SQLModel, col, select
     8→
     9→from adapter_registry.exceptions import AdapterAlreadyExistsError, AdapterNotFoundError
    10→from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord
    11→
    12→
    13→class AdapterRegistry:
    14→    """Registry for storing and querying LoRA adapter metadata.
    15→
    16→    Provides CRUD operations backed by SQLite via SQLModel. The registry
    17→    is initialized with a SQLAlchemy Engine and creates tables idempotently
    18→    on construction. WAL mode is activated automatically on every new
    19→    SQLite connection via an event hook registered before table creation.
    20→
    21→    Raises:
    22→        AdapterAlreadyExistsError: When storing a duplicate adapter ID.
    23→        AdapterNotFoundError: When querying for a non-existent adapter.
    24→
    25→    Example:
    26→        >>> from sqlalchemy import create_engine
    27→        >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
    28→        >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    29→        >>> registry.store(record)
    30→    """
    31→
    32→    def __init__(self, engine: Engine) -> None:
    33→        """Initialize the registry with a SQLAlchemy Engine.
    34→
    35→        Registers a WAL-mode hook on the engine before creating tables to
    36→        ensure every connection (including those opened by create_all) uses
    37→        Write-Ahead Logging. Table creation is idempotent — safe to call
    38→        against an existing database.
    39→
    40→        Args:
    41→            engine: A SQLAlchemy Engine connected to the target SQLite database.
    42→        """
    43→        self._engine = engine
    44→
    45→        @event.listens_for(engine, "connect")
    46→        def _set_wal(dbapi_conn, _record):  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    47→            dbapi_conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
    48→
    49→        SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)
    50→
    51→    def store(self, record: AdapterRecord) -> None:
    52→        """Store a new adapter record in the registry.
    53→
    54→        Args:
    55→            record: The AdapterRecord to persist.
    56→
    57→        Raises:
    58→            AdapterAlreadyExistsError: If an adapter with the same ID exists.
    59→
    60→        Example:
    61→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
    62→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    63→            >>> record = AdapterRecord(id="abc-1", version=1, task_type="bug-fix",
    64→            ...     base_model_id="qwen2.5-coder-7b", rank=16,
    65→            ...     created_at="2026-01-01T00:00:00Z",
    66→            ...     file_path="/adapters/abc-1.safetensors",
    67→            ...     file_hash="sha256hash", file_size_bytes=4096,
    68→            ...     source="distillation", session_id="sess-001")
    69→            >>> registry.store(record)
    70→        """
    71→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
    72→            if session.get(AdapterRecord, record.id) is not None:
    73→                raise AdapterAlreadyExistsError(
    74→                    f"Adapter '{record.id}' already exists."
    75→                )
    76→            session.add(record)
    77→            session.commit()
    78→            session.expunge(record)
    79→
    80→    def retrieve_by_id(self, adapter_id: str) -> AdapterRecord:
    81→        """Retrieve a single adapter record by its unique ID.
    82→
    83→        Args:
    84→            adapter_id: The unique identifier of the adapter to retrieve.
    85→
    86→        Returns:
    87→            The matching AdapterRecord.
    88→
    89→        Raises:
    90→            AdapterNotFoundError: If no adapter with the given ID exists.
    91→
    92→        Example:
    93→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
    94→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
    95→            >>> record = registry.retrieve_by_id("abc-1")
    96→        """
    97→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
    98→            record = session.get(AdapterRecord, adapter_id)
    99→            if record is None:
   100→                raise AdapterNotFoundError(f"No adapter with id '{adapter_id}'.")
   101→            session.expunge(record)
   102→            return record
   103→
   104→    def query_by_task_type(self, task_type: str) -> list[AdapterRecord]:
   105→        """Query all adapters matching a given task type.
   106→
   107→        Args:
   108→            task_type: The task category to filter by (e.g. 'bug-fix').
   109→
   110→        Returns:
   111→            List of matching AdapterRecord instances. Empty list if none match.
   112→
   113→        Example:
   114→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
   115→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
   116→            >>> results = registry.query_by_task_type("bug-fix")
   117→        """
   118→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   119→            stmt = select(AdapterRecord).where(AdapterRecord.task_type == task_type)
   120→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   121→            for r in results:
   122→                session.expunge(r)
   123→            return results
   124→
   125→    def list_all(self) -> list[AdapterRecord]:
   126→        """List all non-archived adapter records.
   127→
   128→        Returns:
   129→            List of all AdapterRecord instances where is_archived=False.
   130→
   131→        Example:
   132→            >>> engine = create_engine("sqlite:///adapters.db")
   133→            >>> registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=engine)
   134→            >>> all_adapters = registry.list_all()
   135→        """
   136→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   137→            stmt = select(AdapterRecord).where(
   138→                AdapterRecord.is_archived == False  # noqa: E712
   139→            )
   140→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   141→            for r in results:
   142→                session.expunge(r)
   143→            return results
   144→
   145→    def query_best_for_task(
   146→        self, task_type: str, top_k: int = 3
   147→    ) -> list[AdapterRecord]:
   148→        """Return the top-k highest-fitness adapters for a task type.
   149→
   150→        Args:
   151→            task_type: Task category to filter by.
   152→            top_k: Maximum number of results to return.
   153→
   154→        Returns:
   155→            List of AdapterRecord instances ordered by fitness_score DESC.
   156→        """
   157→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   158→            stmt = (
   159→                select(AdapterRecord)
   160→                .where(
   161→                    AdapterRecord.task_type == task_type,
   162→                    AdapterRecord.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   163→                )
   164→                .order_by(col(AdapterRecord.fitness_score).desc())
   165→                .limit(top_k)
   166→            )
   167→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   168→            for r in results:
   169→                session.expunge(r)
   170→            return results
   171→
   172→    def is_task_solved(self, task_hash: str, threshold: float = 0.95) -> bool:
   173→        """Check if any adapter for the given task hash meets the threshold.
   174→
   175→        Args:
   176→            task_hash: Training task hash to check.
   177→            threshold: Minimum pass_rate to consider "solved".
   178→
   179→        Returns:
   180→            True if a qualifying adapter exists.
   181→        """
   182→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   183→            stmt = (
   184→                select(AdapterRecord)
   185→                .where(
   186→                    AdapterRecord.training_task_hash == task_hash,
   187→                    AdapterRecord.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   188→                    col(AdapterRecord.pass_rate) >= threshold,
   189→                )
   190→                .limit(1)
   191→            )
   192→            return session.exec(stmt).first() is not None
   193→
   194→    def get_lineage(self, adapter_id: str) -> list[str]:
   195→        """Walk the parent_ids chain for an adapter.
   196→
   197→        Args:
   198→            adapter_id: Starting adapter ID.
   199→
   200→        Returns:
   201→            List of adapter IDs from the given adapter back to the root.
   202→        """
   203→        chain: list[str] = [adapter_id]
   204→        current_id = adapter_id
   205→        visited: set[str] = {adapter_id}
   206→
   207→        while True:
   208→            with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   209→                record = session.get(AdapterRecord, current_id)
   210→                if record is None or record.parent_ids is None:
   211→                    break
   212→                parent_list: list[str] = json.loads(record.parent_ids)
   213→                if not parent_list:
   214→                    break
   215→                next_id = parent_list[0]
   216→                if next_id in visited:
   217→                    break
   218→                visited.add(next_id)
   219→                chain.append(next_id)
   220→                current_id = next_id
   221→
   222→        return chain
   223→
   224→    def query_unevaluated(self, task_type: str | None = None) -> list[AdapterRecord]:
   225→        """Return adapters that have not been evaluated (pass_rate IS NULL).
   226→
   227→        Args:
   228→            task_type: Optional task type filter.
   229→
   230→        Returns:
   231→            List of unevaluated AdapterRecord instances.
   232→        """
   233→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   234→            stmt = select(AdapterRecord).where(
   235→                AdapterRecord.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   236→                col(AdapterRecord.pass_rate).is_(None),  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   237→            )
   238→            if task_type is not None:
   239→                stmt = stmt.where(AdapterRecord.task_type == task_type)
   240→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   241→            for r in results:
   242→                session.expunge(r)
   243→            return results
   244→
   245→    def archive(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:
   246→        """Archive an adapter by setting is_archived=True.
   247→
   248→        Args:
   249→            adapter_id: ID of the adapter to archive.
   250→
   251→        Raises:
   252→            AdapterNotFoundError: If no adapter with the given ID exists.
   253→        """
   254→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   255→            record = session.get(AdapterRecord, adapter_id)
   256→            if record is None:
   257→                raise AdapterNotFoundError(f"No adapter with id '{adapter_id}'.")
   258→            record.is_archived = True
   259→            session.add(record)
   260→            session.commit()
   261→
   262→    def get_task_types(self) -> list[str]:
   263→        """Return all distinct task types in the registry.
   264→
   265→        Returns:
   266→            Sorted list of unique task_type values.
   267→        """
   268→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   269→            stmt = select(AdapterRecord.task_type).distinct()
   270→            results = list(session.exec(stmt).all())
   271→            return sorted(results)
   272→
   273→    def update_fitness(
   274→        self,
   275→        adapter_id: str,
   276→        pass_rate: float,
   277→        fitness_score: float,
   278→    ) -> None:
   279→        """Update evaluation metrics for an adapter.
   280→
   281→        Args:
   282→            adapter_id: ID of the adapter to update.
   283→            pass_rate: New pass rate value.
   284→            fitness_score: New fitness score value.
   285→
   286→        Raises:
   287→            AdapterNotFoundError: If no adapter with the given ID exists.
   288→        """
   289→        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:
   290→            record = session.get(AdapterRecord, adapter_id)
   291→            if record is None:
   292→                raise AdapterNotFoundError(f"No adapter with id '{adapter_id}'.")
   293→            record.pass_rate = pass_rate
   294→            record.fitness_score = fitness_score
   295→            session.add(record)
   296→            session.commit()
   297→

     2→
     3→Module-level JOB_STORE dict shared across all request handlers.
     4→State is lost on service restart — acceptable for single-user local MVP.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from dataclasses import dataclass
    10→from typing import Optional
    11→
    12→JOB_STORE: dict[str, "JobStatus"] = {}
    13→
    14→
    15→@dataclass
    16→class JobStatus:
    17→    """Training job status tracker."""

    21→def calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples: int, n_correct: int, k: int = 1) -> float:
    22→    """Calculate the Pass@k metric for code generation evaluation.
    23→
    24→    Computes the probability that at least one of k sampled solutions is
    25→    correct, using the unbiased estimator from the HumanEval paper
    26→    (Chen et al., 2021). This avoids the high-variance naive estimator.
    27→
    28→    Formula: 1 - prod((n-c-i)/(n-i) for i in range(k))
    29→
    30→    Args:
    31→        n_samples: Total number of samples generated per problem.
    32→        n_correct: Number of correct samples out of n_samples.
    33→        k: Number of attempts allowed for the Pass@k metric. Defaults to 1.
    34→
    35→    Returns:
    36→        Pass@k probability as a float between 0.0 and 1.0.
    37→
    38→    Raises:
    39→        ValueError: If n_correct > n_samples.
    40→
    41→    Example:
    42→        >>> score = calculate_pass_at_k(n_samples=100, n_correct=85, k=1)
    43→        >>> score
    44→        0.85
    45→    """
    46→    if n_correct > n_samples:
    47→        raise ValueError(
    48→            f"n_correct ({n_correct}) cannot be greater than n_samples ({n_samples})"
    49→        )
    50→    if n_correct == n_samples:

     1→"""Out-of-distribution benchmark for adapter generalization testing.
     2→
     3→Provides functions for evaluating adapter performance on tasks outside
     4→the training distribution, measuring generalization capability.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import json
    10→import subprocess
    11→import sys
    12→import tempfile
    13→from pathlib import Path
    14→from typing import Any
    15→
    16→_OOD_DATA_DIR = Path(__file__).parent / "data"
    17→
    18→
    19→def run_ood_benchmark(
    20→    adapter_id: str | None,
    21→    completions: dict[str, str],
    22→    benchmark_name: str = "ood_python",
    23→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    24→    """Run an out-of-distribution benchmark on provided completions.
    25→
    26→    Evaluates completions against OOD tasks from the bundled task set.
    27→    Each task's prompt + completion is executed in a subprocess with its
    28→    test harness.
    29→
    30→    Args:
    31→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter being tested (informational).
    32→        completions: Dict mapping task_id to completion string.
    33→        benchmark_name: Name of the OOD benchmark set.
    34→
    35→    Returns:
    36→        Dictionary with ood_pass_rate and per-task results.
    37→    """
    38→    ood_path = _OOD_DATA_DIR / "ood_tasks.json"
    39→    with ood_path.open() as f:
    40→        all_tasks: list[dict[str, str]] = json.load(f)
    41→
    42→    task_map = {t["task_id"]: t for t in all_tasks}
    43→    task_results: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    44→
    45→    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
    46→        for task_id, completion in completions.items():
    47→            task = task_map.get(task_id)
    48→            if task is None:
    49→                continue
    50→
    51→            script = task["prompt"] + completion + "\n" + task["test"] + "\n"
    52→            script_path = Path(tmpdir) / f"{task_id.replace('/', '_')}.py"
    53→            script_path.write_text(script)
    54→
    55→            try:
    56→                proc = subprocess.run(
    57→                    [sys.executable, str(script_path)],
    58→                    capture_output=True,
    59→                    text=True,
    60→                    timeout=30,
    61→                    cwd=tmpdir,
    62→                )
    63→                passed = proc.returncode == 0
    64→            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    65→                passed = False
    66→
    67→            task_results.append({"task_id": task_id, "passed": passed})
    68→
    69→    total = len(task_results)
    70→    pass_count = sum(1 for r in task_results if r["passed"])
    71→    ood_pass_rate = pass_count / total if total > 0 else 0.0
    72→
    73→    return {
    74→        "adapter_id": adapter_id,
    75→        "benchmark_name": benchmark_name,
    76→        "ood_pass_rate": ood_pass_rate,
    77→        "pass_count": pass_count,
    78→        "total": total,
    79→        "task_results": task_results,
    80→    }
    81→
    82→
    83→def compute_generalization_delta(
    84→    in_dist_rate: float,
    85→    ood_rate: float,
    86→) -> float:
    87→    """Compute the generalization delta between in-distribution and OOD performance.
    88→
    89→    A positive delta means the adapter does better on OOD tasks relative to
    90→    in-distribution performance. A negative delta indicates overfitting.
    91→
    92→    Args:
    93→        in_dist_rate: Pass rate on in-distribution tasks (0.0 to 1.0).
    94→        ood_rate: Pass rate on OOD tasks (0.0 to 1.0).
    95→
    96→    Returns:
    97→        Generalization delta as ood_rate - in_dist_rate.
    98→    """
    99→    return ood_rate - in_dist_rate
   100→

     2→
     3→The hypernetwork carries the ENTIRE project lifecycle state through weight-space.
     4→The prompt is the launch trigger and stays constant. Each iteration's output
     5→(plan, code, test results) gets encoded into an adapter via hypernetwork, and

     7→
     8→Iteration 0 (bootstrap):

    10→
    11→Iteration 1 (planning):
    12→  project prompt + methodology_adapter → plan output → H() → plan_adapter
    13→
    14→Iteration 2+ (coding/fixing):
    15→  project prompt + latest_adapter → code → execute → H() → next_adapter

    17→Usage:

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Fix All Code Review Issues

## Context
A code review identified 34 issues across 8 categories in the Rune monorepo: silent failures, DRY violations, design problems, clean code, and correctness issues. The goal is to fix all of them such that `ruff format`, `ruff check`, `mypy`, and `pytest` all pass. All changes must be backward-compatible or update all call sites.

---

## Checklist (34 issues)

### CRITICAL — Silent Failures
- [ ] #1 `checkpoint_db.py` — log warning in `mark_completed`/`mark_failed` when no running record found
- [ ] #2 `hardware.py` — narrow `except (ImportError, Exception): pass` to `except ImportError` + `except Exception as e: logger.debug(…)`
- [ ] #3 `transformers_provider.py` — fix `dtype=` → `torch_dtype=`
- [ ] #4 `llamacpp_provider.py` — raise `ValueError` when `adapter_id` provided but not loaded
- [ ] #5 `transformers_provider.py` + `_activate_adapter` — raise `ValueError` instead of silent return
- [ ] #6 `training.py` — store `traceback.format_exc()` in error field; add `logger.exception(…)`
- [ ] #7 `lora-server/health.py` — log the caught exception before returning `False`
- [ ] #8 `merging.py` — raise `ValueError` for empty `state_dicts` in both `ties_merge` and `dare_merge`
- [ ] #9-sw `swarm_workers.py` — add `exc_info=True` to sleep/wake failure warnings (re-raise not appropriate since it's fire-and-forget)

### HIGH — DRY Violations
- [ ] #9-storage Extract shared `create_service_engine(default_url)` to `libs/shared/src/shared/storage_utils.py`; update all 3 `storage.py` files
- [ ] #10 `registry.py` — add `_execute_query()` helper to eliminate session/expunge boilerplate
- [ ] #11 Extract WAL pragma to `shared.storage_utils.set_wal_mode(engine)`; update `registry.py` and `checkpoint_db.py`
- [ ] #12 FastAPI app factory — after reading main.py files; extract `create_app()` if pattern exists
- [ ] #13 Create `scripts/bootstrap.py` with `setup_path()`; replace 10-line sys.path block in all scripts
- [ ] #14 Extract `safe_subprocess_run(script_path, cwd, timeout)` helper in `libs/evaluation/`; use in `metrics.py` and `ood_benchmark.py`
- [ ] #15 Add `get_best_device()` to `shared.hardware` (cuda > mps > cpu); use in `e2e_sakana.py` and `e2e_test.py`

### HIGH — Design Problems
- [ ] #16 `training_svc/jobs.py` — add `threading.Lock` protecting all `JOB_STORE` reads/writes
- [ ] #17 `checkpoint_db.py` — add `run_id: str = "default"` constructor parameter; propagate to `mark_running`, `mark_completed`, `mark_failed`
- [ ] #18 `training.py` — validate `request.trajectory_ids` is non-empty before `[0]` access; return HTTP 422
- [ ] #19 `training.py` — sanitize `adapter_id` to reject path-separator chars (`/`, `..`)
- [ ] #20 `rune_agent/graph.py` — fix `get_graph()` singleton with `threading.Lock`
- [ ] #21 `training.py` — assign `adapter_id` at job creation time for hypernetwork jobs (not inside worker)
- [ ] #22 Add `TaskStatus(str, Enum)` to `libs/shared/src/shared/rune_models.py`; use in `checkpoint_db.py`, `jobs.py`, `nodes.py`
- [ ] #23 `conftest.py` — add `_make_factory(cls, defaults)` helper; refactor all 7 fixtures to use it

### MEDIUM — Clean Code
- [ ] #24 Investigate `VLLMProvider` for `lora_name` vs `adapter_id` inconsistency; standardize
- [ ] #25 Rename `_ensure_model()` → `_load_model_if_needed()` in both providers
- [ ] #30 Replace magic numbers with named constants:
  - `llamacpp_provider.py` — `STOP_SEQUENCES = ["```\n", "\n\n\n"]`
  - `hardware.py` — `VRAM_PER_LORA_MB = 100`, `MIN_TRAINING_VRAM_MB = 4096`
  - `hypernetwork.py:254` — `DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE = 32000`
  - `swarm.py` — `MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS = 30`

### MEDIUM — Correctness
- [ ] #31 `swarm.py:100` — replace `uuid.uuid4()` fallback with deterministic content hash
- [ ] #32 `swarm_workers.py` — snapshot `agents` list before operating in watchdog (asyncio TOCTOU)
- [ ] #33 `lora-server/config.py` — guard `yaml.safe_load()` against `None` (empty file)
- [ ] #34 `rune_agent/nodes.py` — align `DEFAULT_MODEL` to `"Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct"` (match training default)

---

```

- Line 75: `dtype=` → `torch_dtype=`
- `generate()` lines 105-108: raise `ValueError` if `adapter_id` provided but not in `_loaded_adapters`
- `_activate_adapter()` line 141: raise `ValueError` instead of silent return

- Add `run_id: str = "default"` to `__init__`; store as `self._run_id`; use in all `mark_*` methods

- `mark_failed` else-branch: same pattern
- Use `set_wal_mode(engine)` from storage_utils

**H. `libs/model-training/src/model_training/merging.py`**

- `train_hypernetwork` endpoint: validate `request.trajectory_ids` non-empty; raise `HTTPException(422, "trajectory_ids must not be empty")`

**N. `scripts/swarm_workers.py`**

- `_wake_vllm` except: same

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/storage_utils.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Shared SQLAlchemy/SQLite utilities for all Rune services.\n\nProvides:\n- set_wal_mode: register WAL-mode PRAGMA on a SQLAlchemy Engine (DRY for all DBs)\n- create_service_engine: build an Engine from DATABASE_URL env or a default URL\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import event\nfrom sqlalchemy.engine import Engine\nfrom sqlmodel import create_engine\n\n\ndef set_wal_mode(engine: Engine) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Register a SQLAlchemy connect event that enables WAL journal mode.\n\n    WAL mode allows concurrent readers while a writer is active, which is\n    important for swarm workloads that read checkpoints while writing.\n    Safe to call multiple times on different engines; each call registers\n    a separate listener on its own engine instance.\n\n    Args:\n        engine: SQLAlchemy Engine to configure.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @event.listens_for(engine, \"connect\")\n    def _set_wal(dbapi_conn: object, _record: object) -> None:  # type: ignore[type-arg]\n        assert hasattr(dbapi_conn, \"execute\")\n        dbapi_conn.execute(\"PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL\")  # type: ignore[union-attr]\n\n\ndef create_service_engine(default_url: str) -> Engine:\n    \"\"\"Create a SQLAlchemy Engine from DATABASE_URL env var or a default URL.\n\n    All three Rune services (api-service, training-svc, evolution-svc) use the\n    same pattern: read DATABASE_URL from the environment, falling back to a\n    service-specific SQLite file. This helper centralises that pattern.\n\n    Args:\n        default_url: Fallback DB URL when DATABASE_URL env var is not set.\n            Typically a SQLite path, e.g. ``\"sqlite:///training_svc.db\"``.\n\n    Returns:\n        A configured SQLAlchemy Engine.\n\n    Example:\n        >>> engine = create_service_engine(\"sqlite:///training_svc.db\")\n    \"\"\"\n    db_url = os.getenv(\"DATABASE_URL\", default_url)\n    connect_args = {\"check_same_thread\": False} if db_url.startswith(\"sqlite\") else {}\n    return create_engine(db_url, connect_args=connect_args, echo=False)\n"
}

  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/evaluation/src/evaluation/utils.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Shared subprocess execution utilities for evaluation benchmarks.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n\ndef safe_subprocess_run(\n    script_path: Path,\n    cwd: str,\n    timeout: int = 30,\n) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Run a Python script in a subprocess and return whether it passed.\n\n    Handles ``subprocess.TimeoutExpired`` by returning ``False``.\n    Used by both HumanEval and OOD benchmark runners to avoid duplicated\n    try/except + subprocess.run boilerplate.\n\n    Args:\n        script_path: Path to the Python script to execute.\n        cwd: Working directory for the subprocess.\n        timeout: Maximum execution time in seconds before the process is killed.\n\n    Returns:\n        ``True`` if the process exits with return code 0, ``False`` otherwise\n        (including timeout).\n\n    Example:\n        >>> from pathlib import Path\n        >>> passed = safe_subprocess_run(Path(\"/tmp/test.py\"), cwd=\"/tmp\")\n        >>> isinstance(passed, bool)\n        True\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        proc = subprocess.run(\n            [sys.executable, str(script_path)],\n            capture_output=True,\n            text=True,\n            timeout=timeout,\n            cwd=cwd,\n        )\n        return proc.returncode == 0\n    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n        return False\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"Bootstrap: add all workspace source directories to sys.path.\n\nImport this module at the top of any standalone script that needs to import\nfrom workspace packages without a full ``uv run`` invocation.\n\nUsage::\n\n    import sys\n    from pathlib import Path\n    sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))\n    from bootstrap import setup_path; setup_path()\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n_WORKSPACE_SRC_DIRS = [\n    \"services/rune-agent/src\",\n    \"services/training-svc/src\",\n    \"services/evolution-svc/src\",\n    \"services/api-service/src\",\n    \"libs/inference/src\",\n    \"libs/shared/src\",\n    \"libs/model-training/src\",\n    \"libs/adapter-registry/src\",\n    \"libs/evaluation/src\",\n]\n\n\ndef setup_path() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Insert all workspace src directories into ``sys.path`` (idempotent).\n\n    Determines the workspace root as the parent of the ``scripts/`` directory,\n    then prepends each ``src`` dir to ``sys.path`` if not already present.\n    \"\"\"\n    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\n    for rel_src in _WORKSPACE_SRC_DIRS:\n        p = str(root / rel_src)\n        if p not in sys.path:\n            sys.path.insert(0, p)\n"
}

     1→"""Rune-specific Pydantic models for cross-service data contracts.
     2→
     3→Defines the shared data shapes for coding sessions, adapter references,
     4→and evolutionary fitness metrics used across all Rune services.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Optional
     8→
     9→from pydantic import BaseModel
    10→
    11→
    12→class AdapterRef(BaseModel):
    13→    """Reference to a stored LoRA adapter.
    14→
    15→    Used within CodingSession to track which adapters were loaded
    16→    during an agent coding session.
    17→
    18→    Attributes:
    19→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter in the registry.
    20→        task_type: Task category this adapter was trained on (e.g. 'bug-fix').
    21→        fitness_score: Evolutionary fitness score, if evaluated.
    22→
    23→    Example:
    24→        >>> ref = AdapterRef(adapter_id="abc-123", task_type="bug-fix")
    25→        >>> ref.adapter_id
    26→        'abc-123'
    27→        >>> ref.fitness_score is None
    28→        True
    29→    """
    30→
    31→    adapter_id: str
    32→    task_type: str
    33→    fitness_score: Optional[float] = None
    34→
    35→
    36→class CodingSession(BaseModel):
    37→    """A complete agent coding session record.
    38→
    39→    Tracks the full lifecycle of a Rune agent session including which
    40→    adapters were used, how many generate-execute-reflect cycles occurred,
    41→    and the final outcome.
    42→
    43→    Attributes:
    44→        session_id: Unique session identifier.
    45→        task_description: Human-readable description of the coding task.
    46→        task_type: Task category (e.g. 'bug-fix', 'feature-impl').
    47→        adapter_refs: List of adapters loaded during this session.
    48→        attempt_count: Number of generate-execute-reflect cycles completed.
    49→        outcome: Final session result ('success', 'exhausted', or None if in progress).
    50→
    51→    Example:
    52→        >>> session = CodingSession(
    53→        ...     session_id="sess-001",
    54→        ...     task_description="Fix import error",
    55→        ...     task_type="bug-fix",
    56→        ... )
    57→        >>> session.adapter_refs
    58→        []
    59→        >>> session.attempt_count
    60→        0
    61→        >>> session.outcome is None
    62→        True
    63→    """
    64→
    65→    session_id: str
    66→    task_description: str
    67→    task_type: str
    68→    adapter_refs: list[AdapterRef] = []
    69→    attempt_count: int = 0
    70→    outcome: Optional[str] = None
    71→
    72→
    73→class EvolMetrics(BaseModel):
    74→    """Evolution evaluation metrics for an adapter.
    75→
    76→    Captures the performance and fitness measurements used by the
    77→    evolution service to decide which adapters to keep, mutate, or retire.
    78→
    79→    Attributes:
    80→        adapter_id: UUID of the adapter being evaluated.
    81→        pass_rate: Pass rate on benchmark tasks (0.0 to 1.0).
    82→        fitness_score: Overall evolutionary fitness score.
    83→        generalization_delta: Difference between in-distribution and OOD performance.
    84→
    85→    Example:
    86→        >>> metrics = EvolMetrics(
    87→        ...     adapter_id="adapter-001",
    88→        ...     pass_rate=0.85,
    89→        ...     fitness_score=0.9,
    90→        ... )
    91→        >>> metrics.generalization_delta is None
    92→        True
    93→    """
    94→
    95→    adapter_id: str
    96→    pass_rate: float
    97→    fitness_score: float
    98→    generalization_delta: Optional[float] = None
    99→
   100→
   101→class SwarmConfig(BaseModel):
   102→    """Configuration for a swarm execution run.
   103→
   104→    Attributes:
   105→        db_url: SQLite database URL for the adapter registry.
   106→        task_source: Path to task definitions file or inline task list.
   107→        population_size: Number of concurrent swarm agents.
   108→        max_generations: Maximum evolutionary generations.
   109→        evolution_interval: Seconds between evolution sweeps.
   110→        sandbox_backend: Execution backend ('subprocess' or 'nsjail').
   111→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model identifier for inference.
   112→        hypernetwork_checkpoint: Path to pretrained hypernetwork checkpoint.
   113→    """
   114→
   115→    db_url: str = "sqlite:///rune_swarm.db"
   116→    task_source: str = "tasks.json"
   117→    population_size: int = 8
   118→    max_generations: int = 10
   119→    evolution_interval: int = 7200
   120→    sandbox_backend: str = "subprocess"
   121→    base_model_id: str = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B"
   122→    hypernetwork_checkpoint: str | None = None
   123→
   124→
   125→class SwarmCheckpoint(BaseModel):
   126→    """Status record for a single swarm task execution.
   127→
   128→    Attributes:
   129→        run_id: Unique identifier for the swarm run.

   136→    """
   137→
   138→    run_id: str
   139→    task_hash: str
   140→    agent_id: str
   141→    status: str = "pending"
   142→    outcome: Optional[str] = None
   143→    started_at: Optional[str] = None
   144→    completed_at: Optional[str] = None
   145→

  "old_string": "\"\"\"Rune-specific Pydantic models for cross-service data contracts.\n\nDefines the shared data shapes for coding sessions, adapter references,\nand evolutionary fitness metrics used across all Rune services.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel",
  "new_string": "\"\"\"Rune-specific Pydantic models for cross-service data contracts.\n\nDefines the shared data shapes for coding sessions, adapter references,\nand evolutionary fitness metrics used across all Rune services.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom enum import Enum\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel\n\n\nclass TaskStatus(str, Enum):\n    \"\"\"Canonical status strings used across services and swarm checkpoints.\n\n    Inherits from ``str`` so values are JSON-serialisable and can be compared\n    directly to string literals in existing code (e.g. ``record.status == \"running\"``).\n\n    Example:\n        >>> TaskStatus.RUNNING\n        <TaskStatus.RUNNING: 'running'>\n        >>> TaskStatus.RUNNING == \"running\"\n        True\n        >>> \"running\" == TaskStatus.RUNNING\n        True\n    \"\"\"\n\n    PENDING = \"pending\"\n    QUEUED = \"queued\"\n    RUNNING = \"running\"\n    COMPLETED = \"completed\"\n    FAILED = \"failed\"\n    SUCCESS = \"success\"\n    EXHAUSTED = \"exhausted\""
}

  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/inference/src/inference/transformers_provider.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TransformersProvider: InferenceProvider using HuggingFace transformers + PEFT.\n\nLoads models via AutoModelForCausalLM and applies LoRA adapters via PEFT.\nThis is the only provider that natively supports PEFT-format adapters\n(safetensors) as output by the hypernetwork.\n\nIMPORTANT: transformers, torch, and peft are imported inside method bodies\nper INFRA-05 pattern so that this module is importable in CPU-only CI.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom inference.provider import GenerationResult, InferenceProvider\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\nclass TransformersProvider(InferenceProvider):\n    \"\"\"InferenceProvider backed by HuggingFace transformers with PEFT LoRA.\n\n    Loads models locally via AutoModelForCausalLM. Adapters are applied\n    via PEFT's PeftModel, which natively reads the safetensors format\n    output by the hypernetwork.\n\n    Args:\n        model_name: HuggingFace model ID or local path.\n        device: Device to load model onto ('cpu', 'mps', 'cuda').\n        torch_dtype: Model dtype ('auto', 'float16', 'bfloat16').\n\n    Example:\n        >>> provider = TransformersProvider(model_name=\"Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-0.5B\")\n        >>> result = await provider.generate(\"def hello\", model=\"ignored\")\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        model_name: str = \"\",\n        device: str = \"cpu\",\n        torch_dtype: str = \"auto\",\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize TransformersProvider.\n\n        Args:\n            model_name: HuggingFace model ID or local path.\n            device: Device to load model onto.\n            torch_dtype: Model dtype string.\n        \"\"\"\n        self._model_name = model_name\n        self._device = device\n        self._torch_dtype = torch_dtype\n        self._model: Any = None\n        self._tokenizer: Any = None\n        self._base_model: Any = None\n        self._loaded_adapters: dict[str, str] = {}  # id -> path\n        self._active_adapter: str | None = None\n\n    def _load_model_if_needed(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Load the base model and tokenizer if not already loaded.\"\"\"\n        if self._model is not None:\n            return\n\n        from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        logger.info(\"Loading model: %s (device=%s)\", self._model_name, self._device)\n\n        self._tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(self._model_name)\n        if self._tokenizer.pad_token is None:\n            self._tokenizer.pad_token = self._tokenizer.eos_token\n\n        self._model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n            self._model_name,\n            torch_dtype=self._torch_dtype,\n        )\n        self._model.to(self._device)\n        self._model.eval()\n        self._base_model = self._model\n        logger.info(\"Model loaded: %s\", self._model_name)\n\n    async def generate(\n        self,\n        prompt: str,\n        model: str,\n        adapter_id: str | None = None,\n        max_tokens: int = 1024,\n    ) -> GenerationResult:\n        \"\"\"Generate text using transformers with optional PEFT adapter.\n\n        Args:\n            prompt: The input prompt.\n            model: Ignored (model is set at construction).\n            adapter_id: LoRA adapter ID to activate. Must be loaded via\n                load_adapter() before use.\n            max_tokens: Maximum tokens to generate.\n\n        Returns:\n            GenerationResult with generated text and metadata.\n\n        Raises:\n            ValueError: If adapter_id is provided but has not been loaded.\n        \"\"\"\n        import torch  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        self._load_model_if_needed()\n\n        # Validate adapter before switching\n        if adapter_id and adapter_id not in self._loaded_adapters:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"Adapter '{adapter_id}' has not been loaded. \"\n                \"Call load_adapter() first.\"\n            )\n\n        # Switch adapter if needed\n        if adapter_id and adapter_id != self._active_adapter:\n            self._activate_adapter(adapter_id)\n        elif not adapter_id and self._active_adapter:\n            self._deactivate_adapter()\n\n        inputs = self._tokenizer(\n            prompt, return_tensors=\"pt\", truncation=True, max_length=2048\n        )\n        inputs = {k: v.to(self._device) for k, v in inputs.items()}\n        input_len = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            outputs = self._model.generate(\n                **inputs,\n                max_new_tokens=max_tokens,\n                do_sample=True,\n                temperature=0.7,\n                top_p=0.9,\n                pad_token_id=self._tokenizer.pad_token_id,\n            )\n\n        new_tokens = outputs[0][input_len:]\n        text = self._tokenizer.decode(new_tokens, skip_special_tokens=True)\n        total_tokens = outputs.shape[1]\n\n        return GenerationResult(\n            text=text,\n            model=self._model_name,\n            adapter_id=self._active_adapter,\n            token_count=total_tokens,\n            finish_reason=\"stop\",\n        )\n\n    def _activate_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Activate a loaded PEFT adapter.\n\n        Args:\n            adapter_id: The adapter name to activate. Must already be in\n                ``_loaded_adapters``.\n\n        Raises:\n            ValueError: If the adapter has not been loaded.\n        \"\"\"\n        if adapter_id not in self._loaded_adapters:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"Adapter '{adapter_id}' has not been loaded. \"\n                \"Call load_adapter() first.\"\n            )\n\n        from peft import PeftModel  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        adapter_path = self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]\n\n        if self._active_adapter:\n            # Already has a PEFT wrapper — load additional adapter\n            self._model.load_adapter(adapter_path, adapter_name=adapter_id)\n            self._model.set_adapter(adapter_id)\n        else:\n            # First adapter — wrap base model with PeftModel\n            self._model = PeftModel.from_pretrained(\n                self._base_model, adapter_path, adapter_name=adapter_id\n            )\n            self._model.to(self._device)\n            self._model.eval()\n\n        self._active_adapter = adapter_id\n        logger.info(\"Activated adapter: %s\", adapter_id)\n\n    def _deactivate_adapter(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Deactivate current adapter, revert to base model.\"\"\"\n        if self._active_adapter and self._base_model is not None:\n            self._model = self._base_model\n            self._active_adapter = None\n            logger.info(\"Deactivated adapter, reverted to base model\")\n\n    async def load_adapter(self, adapter_id: str, adapter_path: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Register a PEFT adapter directory for use during generation.\n\n        The adapter directory must contain adapter_model.safetensors and\n        adapter_config.json as output by save_hypernetwork_adapter().\n\n        Args:\n            adapter_id: Unique name for the adapter.\n            adapter_path: Path to the PEFT adapter directory.\n        \"\"\"\n        self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id] = adapter_path\n        logger.info(\"Registered adapter %s -> %s\", adapter_id, adapter_path)\n\n    async def unload_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Remove a registered adapter.\n\n        Args:\n            adapter_id: The adapter name to remove.\n        \"\"\"\n        if adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:\n            if self._active_adapter == adapter_id:\n                self._deactivate_adapter()\n            del self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]\n            logger.info(\"Unloaded adapter %s\", adapter_id)\n\n    async def list_adapters(self) -> list[str]:\n        \"\"\"List all registered adapter IDs.\n\n        Returns:\n            Sorted list of registered adapter IDs.\n        \"\"\"\n        return sorted(self._loaded_adapters.keys())\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/inference/src/inference/llamacpp_provider.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"LlamaCppProvider: InferenceProvider using llama-cpp-python with LoRA support.\n\nLoads GGUF models via llama_cpp.Llama with optional LoRA adapter paths.\nDesigned for Apple Silicon (Metal) local inference where adapter hot-loading\nis needed — Ollama cannot load LoRA adapters, and vLLM requires a server.\n\nIMPORTANT: llama_cpp is imported inside method bodies per INFRA-05 pattern\nso that this module is importable in CPU-only CI without llama-cpp-python.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom inference.provider import GenerationResult, InferenceProvider\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# Stop sequences used when generating code completions.\n_STOP_SEQUENCES: list[str] = [\"```\\n\", \"\\n\\n\\n\"]\n\n\nclass LlamaCppProvider(InferenceProvider):\n    \"\"\"InferenceProvider backed by llama-cpp-python with native LoRA support.\n\n    Unlike OllamaProvider, this loads GGUF models directly and can apply\n    LoRA adapters at load time. Unlike VLLMProvider, no server is needed.\n\n    The model is loaded lazily on first generate() call. When an adapter\n    is loaded, the model is reloaded with the LoRA path applied.\n\n    Args:\n        model_path: Path to the GGUF model file.\n        n_ctx: Context window size. Default: 4096.\n        n_gpu_layers: Layers to offload to GPU (-1 = all). Default: -1.\n\n    Example:\n        >>> provider = LlamaCppProvider(model_path=\"/models/qwen2.5-coder-1.5b.gguf\")\n        >>> result = await provider.generate(\"def hello\", model=\"ignored\")\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        model_path: str | None = None,\n        n_ctx: int = 4096,\n        n_gpu_layers: int = -1,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize LlamaCppProvider with model configuration.\n\n        Args:\n            model_path: Path to the GGUF model file.\n            n_ctx: Context window size. Default: 4096.\n            n_gpu_layers: Layers to offload to GPU (-1 = all). Default: -1.\n        \"\"\"\n        self._model_path = model_path or \"\"\n        self._n_ctx = n_ctx\n        self._n_gpu_layers = n_gpu_layers\n        self._llm: Any = None\n        self._current_lora: str | None = None\n        self._loaded_adapters: dict[str, str] = {}  # id -> path\n\n    def _load_model_if_needed(self, lora_path: str | None = None) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Load or reload the GGUF model, optionally with a LoRA adapter.\n\n        Args:\n            lora_path: Path to LoRA adapter file. If different from current,\n                triggers a model reload.\n        \"\"\"\n        if self._llm is not None and lora_path == self._current_lora:\n            return\n\n        from llama_cpp import Llama  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        if self._llm is not None:\n            del self._llm\n            self._llm = None\n\n        logger.info(\n            \"Loading GGUF model: %s (lora=%s, n_ctx=%d, n_gpu_layers=%d)\",\n            self._model_path,\n            lora_path,\n            self._n_ctx,\n            self._n_gpu_layers,\n        )\n\n        kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {\n            \"model_path\": self._model_path,\n            \"n_ctx\": self._n_ctx,\n            \"n_gpu_layers\": self._n_gpu_layers,\n            \"verbose\": False,\n        }\n        if lora_path:\n            kwargs[\"lora_path\"] = lora_path\n\n        self._llm = Llama(**kwargs)\n        self._current_lora = lora_path\n\n    async def generate(\n        self,\n        prompt: str,\n        model: str,\n        adapter_id: str | None = None,\n        max_tokens: int = 1024,\n    ) -> GenerationResult:\n        \"\"\"Generate text using llama-cpp-python with optional LoRA adapter.\n\n        Args:\n            prompt: The input prompt.\n            model: Ignored (model is set at construction via model_path).\n            adapter_id: LoRA adapter ID to apply. Must be loaded via\n                load_adapter() before use.\n            max_tokens: Maximum tokens to generate.\n\n        Returns:\n            GenerationResult with generated text and metadata.\n\n        Raises:\n            ValueError: If adapter_id is provided but has not been loaded.\n        \"\"\"\n        lora_path: str | None = None\n        if adapter_id:\n            if adapter_id not in self._loaded_adapters:\n                raise ValueError(\n                    f\"Adapter '{adapter_id}' has not been loaded. \"\n                    \"Call load_adapter() first.\"\n                )\n            lora_path = self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]\n\n        self._load_model_if_needed(lora_path=lora_path)\n\n        response = self._llm(  # type: ignore[union-attr]\n            prompt,\n            max_tokens=max_tokens,\n            stop=_STOP_SEQUENCES,\n        )\n\n        text = response[\"choices\"][0][\"text\"]\n        token_count = response[\"usage\"][\"total_tokens\"]\n        finish_reason = response[\"choices\"][0].get(\"finish_reason\", \"stop\")\n\n        return GenerationResult(\n            text=text,\n            model=Path(self._model_path).stem,\n            adapter_id=adapter_id,\n            token_count=token_count,\n            finish_reason=finish_reason,\n        )\n\n    async def load_adapter(self, adapter_id: str, adapter_path: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Register a LoRA adapter for use during generation.\n\n        The adapter is applied on next generate() call by reloading the model\n        with the LoRA path. llama-cpp-python applies LoRA at model load time.\n\n        Args:\n            adapter_id: Unique name for the adapter.\n            adapter_path: Filesystem path to the LoRA adapter weights (GGUF format).\n        \"\"\"\n        self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id] = adapter_path\n        logger.info(\"Registered adapter %s -> %s\", adapter_id, adapter_path)\n\n    async def unload_adapter(self, adapter_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Remove a registered LoRA adapter.\n\n        If the currently active adapter is unloaded, the model will reload\n        without it on the next generate() call.\n\n        Args:\n            adapter_id: The adapter name to remove.\n        \"\"\"\n        if adapter_id in self._loaded_adapters:\n            was_active = self._current_lora == self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]\n            del self._loaded_adapters[adapter_id]\n            if was_active:\n                self._current_lora = None  # Force reload without adapter\n            logger.info(\"Unloaded adapter %s\", adapter_id)\n\n    async def list_adapters(self) -> list[str]:\n        \"\"\"List all registered LoRA adapter IDs.\n\n        Returns:\n            Sorted list of registered adapter IDs.\n        \"\"\"\n        return sorted(self._loaded_adapters.keys())\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/checkpoint_db.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"SQLite-backed checkpoint database for swarm task tracking.\n\nProvides SwarmCheckpointDB for recording task execution state across\nswarm agents, enabling crash recovery and deduplication.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom sqlmodel import Field, Session, SQLModel, select\n\nfrom shared.storage_utils import set_wal_mode\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\nclass CheckpointRecord(SQLModel, table=True):\n    \"\"\"Persistent checkpoint record for a swarm task.\n\n    Attributes:\n        id: Auto-incremented primary key.\n        run_id: Swarm run identifier.\n        task_hash: Hash of the task being tracked.\n        agent_id: Agent executing the task.\n        status: Current status (pending, running, completed, failed).\n        outcome: Result description when completed.\n        started_at: ISO 8601 timestamp when execution began.\n        completed_at: ISO 8601 timestamp when execution finished.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    __tablename__ = \"swarm_checkpoints\"\n\n    id: Optional[int] = Field(default=None, primary_key=True)\n    run_id: str\n    task_hash: str = Field(index=True)\n    agent_id: str\n    status: str = \"pending\"\n    outcome: Optional[str] = None\n    started_at: Optional[str] = None\n    completed_at: Optional[str] = None\n\n\nclass SwarmCheckpointDB:\n    \"\"\"SQLite-backed checkpoint database for swarm task tracking.\n\n    Tracks task execution state (pending → running → completed/failed)\n    to enable crash recovery and prevent duplicate work.\n\n    Args:\n        engine: SQLAlchemy Engine connected to the target SQLite database.\n        run_id: Identifier for this swarm run. Stored on every record created\n            by this instance. Defaults to ``\"default\"`` for backward\n            compatibility, but callers should supply a unique run ID so that\n            multiple swarm runs are distinguishable in the database.\n\n    Example:\n        >>> from sqlmodel import create_engine\n        >>> engine = create_engine(\"sqlite:///checkpoints.db\")\n        >>> db = SwarmCheckpointDB(engine, run_id=\"run-2026-03-01\")\n        >>> db.mark_running(\"task-hash-1\", \"agent-01\")\n        >>> db.mark_completed(\"task-hash-1\", \"success: pass_rate=0.95\")\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, engine: object, run_id: str = \"default\") -> None:\n        \"\"\"Initialize checkpoint database and create tables.\n\n        Args:\n            engine: SQLAlchemy Engine connected to the target SQLite database.\n            run_id: Identifier for this swarm run. Defaults to ``\"default\"``.\n        \"\"\"\n        from sqlalchemy.engine import Engine  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        assert isinstance(engine, Engine)\n        self._engine = engine\n        self._run_id = run_id\n\n        set_wal_mode(engine)\n        SQLModel.metadata.create_all(engine)\n\n    def mark_running(self, task_hash: str, agent_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record that an agent has started working on a task.\n\n        Args:\n            task_hash: Hash of the task being started.\n            agent_id: Identifier of the agent executing the task.\n        \"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()\n        record = CheckpointRecord(\n            run_id=self._run_id,\n            task_hash=task_hash,\n            agent_id=agent_id,\n            status=\"running\",\n            started_at=now,\n        )\n        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:\n            session.add(record)\n            session.commit()\n\n    def mark_completed(self, task_hash: str, outcome: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record that a task has been completed.\n\n        Args:\n            task_hash: Hash of the completed task.\n            outcome: Description of the result.\n        \"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()\n        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:\n            stmt = (\n                select(CheckpointRecord)\n                .where(\n                    CheckpointRecord.task_hash == task_hash,\n                    CheckpointRecord.status == \"running\",\n                )\n                .limit(1)\n            )\n            record = session.exec(stmt).first()\n            if record is not None:\n                record.status = \"completed\"\n                record.outcome = outcome\n                record.completed_at = now\n                session.add(record)\n                session.commit()\n            else:\n                # No running record found — this means mark_running() was never\n                # called for this task. Log a warning so the bug is visible,\n                # then create a completed record to keep the DB consistent.\n                logger.warning(\n                    \"mark_completed called for task_hash=%r but no running record \"\n                    \"was found (run_id=%r). mark_running() may not have been called. \"\n                    \"Creating orphaned completed record with agent_id='unknown'.\",\n                    task_hash,\n                    self._run_id,\n                )\n                new_record = CheckpointRecord(\n                    run_id=self._run_id,\n                    task_hash=task_hash,\n                    agent_id=\"unknown\",\n                    status=\"completed\",\n                    outcome=outcome,\n                    completed_at=now,\n                )\n                session.add(new_record)\n                session.commit()\n\n    def mark_failed(self, task_hash: str, agent_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Record that a task execution has failed.\n\n        Args:\n            task_hash: Hash of the failed task.\n            agent_id: Identifier of the agent that failed.\n        \"\"\"\n        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()\n        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:\n            stmt = (\n                select(CheckpointRecord)\n                .where(\n                    CheckpointRecord.task_hash == task_hash,\n                    CheckpointRecord.agent_id == agent_id,\n                    CheckpointRecord.status == \"running\",\n                )\n                .limit(1)\n            )\n            record = session.exec(stmt).first()\n            if record is not None:\n                record.status = \"failed\"\n                record.completed_at = now\n                session.add(record)\n                session.commit()\n            else:\n                # No running record found — log a warning and still create the\n                # failed record so the failure is visible in the DB.\n                logger.warning(\n                    \"mark_failed called for task_hash=%r agent_id=%r but no running \"\n                    \"record was found (run_id=%r). mark_running() may not have been \"\n                    \"called. Creating orphaned failed record.\",\n                    task_hash,\n                    agent_id,\n                    self._run_id,\n                )\n                new_record = CheckpointRecord(\n                    run_id=self._run_id,\n                    task_hash=task_hash,\n                    agent_id=agent_id,\n                    status=\"failed\",\n                    completed_at=now,\n                )\n                session.add(new_record)\n                session.commit()\n\n    def is_completed(self, task_hash: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Check if a task has been completed.\n\n        Args:\n            task_hash: Hash of the task to check.\n\n        Returns:\n            True if the task has a \"completed\" checkpoint record.\n        \"\"\"\n        with Session(self._engine, expire_on_commit=False) as session:\n            stmt = (\n                select(CheckpointRecord)\n                .where(\n                    CheckpointRecord.task_hash == task_hash,\n                    CheckpointRecord.status == \"completed\",\n                )\n                .limit(1)\n            )\n            return session.exec(stmt).first() is not None\n"
}

\"\\\\\", \".\", \"\\x00\"})\n\n\ndef _validate_adapter_id(adapter_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Raise HTTPException(422) if adapter_id contains path-traversal characters.\n\n    Args:\n task_type: str,\n    rank: int,\n    epochs: int,\n    learning_rate: float,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Background worker: runs train_and_register and updates JOB_STORE.\n\n    Import is deferred inside the function body per INFRA-05 (GPU deps\n    should not be imported at service startup in non-GPU environments).\n    \"\"\"\n    with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n        JOB_STORE[job_id].status = \"running\"\n    try:\n        from model_training.trainer import train_and_register  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        train_and_register(\n            session_id=session_id,\n            adapter_id=adapter_id,\n            task_type=task_type,\n            rank=rank,\n            epochs=epochs,\n            learning_rate=learning_rate,\n        )\n        with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].status = \"completed\"\n    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001\n        tb = traceback.format_exc()\n        logger.exception(\"Training job %s failed\", job_id)\n        with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].status = \"failed\"\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].error = f\"{e}\\n\\n{tb}\"\n\n\n@router.post(\"/train/lora\")\nasync def train_lora(\n    request: LoraTrainingRequest,\n    background_tasks: BackgroundTasks,\n) -> JSONResponse:\n    \"\"\"Dispatch a QLoRA training job as a background task.\n\n    Args:\n        request: LoRA training parameters — session_id is required.\n        background_tasks: FastAPI background task runner.\n\n    Returns:\n        JSONResponse with job_id and status=\"queued\".\n\n    Example:\n        >>> body = {\"session_id\": \"s-1\", \"task_type\": \"code-gen\", \"epochs\": 3}\n        >>> response = client.post(\"/train/lora\", json=body)\n        >>> response.status_code\n        200\n        >>> response.json()[\"status\"]\n        'queued'\n    \"\"\"\n    job_id = str(uuid.uuid4())\n    adapter_id = request.adapter_id or str(uuid.uuid4())\n    _validate_adapter_id(adapter_id)\n\n    with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n        JOB_STORE[job_id] = JobStatus(\n            job_id=job_id,\n            status=\"queued\",\n            adapter_id=adapter_id,\n        )\n\n    background_tasks.add_task(\n        _run_training_job,\n        job_id,\n        request.session_id,\n        adapter_id,\n        request.task_type,\n        request.rank,\n        request.epochs,\n        request.learning_rate,\n    )\n\n    return JSONResponse(content={\"job_id\": job_id, \"status\": \"queued\"}, status_code=200)\n\n\ndef _run_hypernetwork_job(\n    job_id: str, adapter_id: str, trajectory_id: str, task_type: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Background worker: runs DocToLoraHypernetwork forward pass and saves adapter.\n\n    Import is deferred inside the function body per INFRA-05 (GPU deps\n    should not be imported at service startup in non-GPU environments).\n\n    Args:\n        job_id: Unique job identifier used to update JOB_STORE.\n        adapter_id: Pre-assigned adapter identifier (assigned at request time).\n        trajectory_id: Trajectory ID to load and tokenize.\n        task_type: Task type string (stored for context, not used in forward pass).\n    \"\"\"\n    with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n        JOB_STORE[job_id].status = \"running\"\n    try:\n        import torch  # noqa: PLC0415\n        from model_training.hypernetwork import (  # noqa: PLC0415\n            DocToLoraHypernetwork,\n            save_hypernetwork_adapter,\n        )\n        from model_training.trajectory import (  # noqa: PLC0415\n            format_for_sft,\n            load_trajectory,\n        )\n        from transformers import AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        # Load and tokenize trajectory\n        trajectory = load_trajectory(trajectory_id)\n        messages = format_for_sft(trajectory)\n        # Concatenate messages into a single text for tokenization\n        text = \" \".join(m[\"content\"] for m in messages)\n\n        # Resolve env vars inside function body for testability\n        base_model_id = os.environ.get(\n            \"RUNE_BASE_MODEL\", \"Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct\"\n        )\n        hypernetwork_weights_path = os.environ.get(\n            \"RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH\",\n            str(Path.home() / \".rune\" / \"hypernetwork.pt\"),\n        )\n        adapter_base = os.environ.get(\"RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR\")\n\n        # Tokenize using base model tokenizer (lightweight, no 7B model loading)\n        tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(base_model_id)\n        token_ids = tokenizer.encode(text, return_tensors=\"pt\")\n\n        # Load hypernetwork and generate adapter\n        hypernetwork = DocToLoraHypernetwork(\n            input_dim=tokenizer.vocab_size,\n        )\n        hypernetwork.load_state_dict(\n            torch.load(hypernetwork_weights_path, map_location=\"cpu\")\n        )\n        hypernetwork.eval()\n\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            weights = hypernetwork(token_ids)\n\n        # Save adapter\n        if adapter_base:\n            adapter_dir = str(Path(adapter_base) / adapter_id)\n        else:\n            adapter_dir = str(Path.home() / \".rune\" / \"adapters\" / adapter_id)\n\n        save_hypernetwork_adapter(\n            weights=weights,\n            output_dir=adapter_dir,\n            base_model_id=base_model_id,\n        )\n\n        # Register adapter in registry (reuse service engine for DB consistency)\n        import hashlib  # noqa: PLC0415\n        from datetime import datetime, timezone  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        from adapter_registry.models import AdapterRecord  # noqa: PLC0415\n        from adapter_registry.registry import AdapterRegistry  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        from training_svc.storage import engine as svc_engine  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        safetensors_path = Path(adapter_dir) / \"adapter_model.safetensors\"\n        file_hash = hashlib.sha256(safetensors_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n        file_size_bytes = safetensors_path.stat().st_size\n\n        registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=svc_engine)\n        record = AdapterRecord(\n            id=adapter_id,\n            version=1,\n            task_type=task_type,\n            base_model_id=base_model_id,\n            rank=8,\n            created_at=datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),\n            file_path=adapter_dir,\n            file_hash=file_hash,\n            file_size_bytes=file_size_bytes,\n            source=\"hypernetwork\",\n            session_id=trajectory_id,\n        )\n        registry.store(record)\n\n        with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].status = \"completed\"\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].adapter_id = adapter_id\n    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001\n        tb = traceback.format_exc()\n        logger.exception(\"Hypernetwork job %s failed\", job_id)\n        with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].status = \"failed\"\n            JOB_STORE[job_id].error = f\"{e}\\n\\n{tb}\"\n\n\n@router.post(\"/train/hypernetwork\")\nasync def train_hypernetwork(\n    request: HypernetworkTrainingRequest,\n    background_tasks: BackgroundTasks,\n) -> JSONResponse:\n    \"\"\"Dispatch a hypernetwork adapter generation job as a background task.\n\n    Accepts a trajectory, runs it through the pre-trained hypernetwork in a\n    single forward pass, saves the adapter in PEFT format, and returns a\n    job_id for status polling via GET /jobs/{job_id}.\n\n    Args:\n        request: Hypernetwork training parameters including task_type and\n            trajectory_ids (uses first trajectory_id).\n        background_tasks: FastAPI background task runner.\n\n    Returns:\n        JSONResponse with job_id and status=\"queued\".\n\n    Raises:\n        HTTPException: 422 if trajectory_ids is empty.\n\n    Example:\n        >>> body = {\"task_type\": \"gen\", \"trajectory_ids\": [\"t-1\"]}\n        >>> response = client.post(\"/train/hypernetwork\", json=body)\n        >>> response.status_code\n        200\n        >>> response.json()[\"status\"]\n        'queued'\n    \"\"\"\n    if not request.trajectory_ids:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=422,\n            detail=\"trajectory_ids must not be empty.\",\n        )\n\n    job_id = str(uuid.uuid4())\n    # Assign adapter_id at creation time so GET /jobs/{id} returns it immediately\n    adapter_id = str(uuid.uuid4())\n\n    with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n        JOB_STORE[job_id] = JobStatus(\n            job_id=job_id,\n            status=\"queued\",\n            adapter_id=adapter_id,\n        )\n\n    background_tasks.add_task(\n        _run_hypernetwork_job,\n        job_id,\n        adapter_id,\n        request.trajectory_ids[0],\n        request.task_type,\n    )\n\n    return JSONResponse(content={\"job_id\": job_id, \"status\": \"queued\"}, status_code=200)\n\n\n@router.get(\"/jobs/{job_id}\")\nasync def get_job_status(job_id: str) -> JSONResponse:\n    \"\"\"Get training job status.\n\n    Args:\n        job_id: Unique identifier for the training job.\n\n    Returns:\n        JSONResponse with job_id, status, adapter_id, and optional error.\n\n    Raises:\n        HTTPException: 404 if job_id not found.\n\n    Example:\n        >>> response = client.get(\"/jobs/job-123\")\n        >>> response.status_code\n        404\n    \"\"\"\n    with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:\n        job = JOB_STORE.get(job_id)\n    if job is None:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f\"Job {job_id} not found\")\n\n    return JSONResponse(\n        content={\n            \"job_id\": job.job_id,\n            \"status\": job.status,\n            \"adapter_id\": job.adapter_id,\n            \"error\": job.error,\n        },\n        status_code=200,\n    )\n"
}

    37→def load_task_pool(task_source: str) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:

    39→
    40→    Args:
    41→        task_source: Path to a JSON file containing task definitions.
    42→
    43→    Returns:

    45→    """

    48→        with path.open() as f:
    49→            return json.load(f)
    50→    logger.warning("Task source %s not found, using empty task pool", task_source)
    51→    return []

   189→async def run_swarm(config: SwarmConfig, dry_run: bool = False) -> None:
   190→    """Tier-1 orchestrator — runs the full swarm with asyncio.TaskGroup.
   191→
   192→    Args:
   193→        config: Swarm configuration.
   194→        dry_run: If True, skip actual execution and training.
   195→    """
   196→    from adapter_registry import AdapterRegistry
   197→    from sqlmodel import create_engine
   198→
   199→    logger.info("Starting swarm with config: %s", config.model_dump())
   200→
   201→    # Detect hardware
   202→    probe = HardwareProbe.detect()
   203→    budget = probe.compute_budget()
   204→    logger.info("Hardware: %s, Budget: %s", probe, budget)

   205→
   206→    # Initialize registry and checkpoint DB
   207→    registry_engine = create_engine(config.db_url)
   208→    registry = AdapterRegistry(engine=registry_engine)
   209→    checkpoint_engine = create_engine("sqlite:///swarm_checkpoints.db")
   210→    checkpoint_db = SwarmCheckpointDB(checkpoint_engine)
   211→
   212→    # Load tasks
   213→    tasks = load_task_pool(config.task_source)
   214→    if not tasks:
   215→        logger.warning("No tasks to process")
   216→        return
   217→
   218→    # Shared state
   219→    training_queue: asyncio.Queue[TrainingRequest] = asyncio.Queue()
   220→    shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
   221→    num_agents = min(config.population_size, budget.max_agents)
   222→
   223→    # Partition tasks across agents
   224→    partitions: list[list[dict[str, Any]]] = [[] for _ in range(num_agents)]
   225→    for i, task in enumerate(tasks):
   226→        partitions[i % num_agents].append(task)
   227→
   228→    use_iteration = bool(config.hypernetwork_checkpoint)
   229→
   230→    async with asyncio.TaskGroup() as tg:
   231→        # Agent supervisors
   232→        agent_tasks = []
   233→        for i in range(num_agents):
   234→            t = tg.create_task(
   235→                agent_supervisor(
   236→                    agent_id=f"agent-{i}",
   237→                    tasks=partitions[i],
   238→                    checkpoint_db=checkpoint_db,
   239→                    training_queue=training_queue,
   240→                    dry_run=dry_run,
   241→                    shutdown_event=shutdown_event,
   242→                    use_iteration_loop=use_iteration,
   243→                    hypernetwork_checkpoint=config.hypernetwork_checkpoint,
   244→                )
   245→            )
   246→            agent_tasks.append(t)
   247→
   248→        # Training pool (background)
   249→        tg.create_task(
   250→            training_pool_manager(
   251→                training_queue=training_queue,
   252→                budget=budget,
   253→                db_url=config.db_url,
   254→                shutdown_event=shutdown_event,
   255→            )
   256→        )
   257→
   258→        # Evolution worker (background)
   259→        tg.create_task(
   260→            evolution_worker(
   261→                registry=registry,
   262→                interval_seconds=config.evolution_interval,
   263→                shutdown_event=shutdown_event,
   264→            )
   265→        )
   266→
   267→        # Memory watchdog (background)
   268→        tg.create_task(
   269→            memory_watchdog(
   270→                budget=budget,
   271→                agents=agent_tasks,
   272→                shutdown_event=shutdown_event,
   273→            )
   274→        )
   275→
   276→        # Wait for all agents to finish, then shutdown background tasks
   277→        done = await asyncio.gather(*agent_tasks, return_exceptions=True)
   278→        shutdown_event.set()
   279→
   280→        for result in done:
   281→            if isinstance(result, Exception):
   282→                logger.error("Agent failed: %s", result)
   283→            else:
   284→                logger.info("Agent result: %s", result)
   285→
   286→
   287→def main() -> None:
   288→    """CLI entry point for the swarm orchestrator."""
   289→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Rune Swarm Orchestrator")
   290→    parser.add_argument(
   291→        "--config",
   292→        default="scripts/swarm_config.yaml",
   293→        help="Path to swarm config YAML",
   294→    )
   295→    parser.add_argument("--agents", type=int, help="Override population_size")
   296→    parser.add_argument("--hours", type=float, help="Max runtime in hours")
   297→    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="Skip execution")
   298→    args = parser.parse_args()
   299→

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/graph.py",
  "old_string": "# Singleton instance for reuse\n_graph = None\n\n\ndef get_graph() -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Get or create the compiled graph instance.\n\n    Returns:\n        Compiled StateGraph instance.\n    \"\"\"\n    global _graph\n    if _graph is None:\n        _graph = create_graph()\n    return _graph",
  "new_string": "# Singleton instance for reuse — guarded by a lock for thread safety.\n_graph: Any = None\n_graph_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n\ndef get_graph() -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Get or create the compiled graph instance (thread-safe singleton).\n\n    Uses double-checked locking so the expensive create_graph() call is only\n    made once, even if multiple threads call get_graph() concurrently.\n\n    Returns:\n        Compiled StateGraph instance.\n    \"\"\"\n    global _graph\n    if _graph is None:\n        with _graph_lock:\n            if _graph is None:\n                _graph = create_graph()\n    return _graph"
}
