Perfect! Now I have a complete understanding of the codebase. Let me compile the comprehensive report:

---

## COMPREHENSIVE HYPERNETWORK IMPLEMENTATION REPORT

Based on my thorough exploration of the rune monorepo, here are ALL the critical details about the hypernetwork implementation:

### 1. **HYPERNETWORK FILE LOCATION**
- **Primary file:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/hypernetwork.py` (404 lines)

### 2. **ARCHITECTURE PATTERN: `_LazyHypernetworkProxy` + `_build_hypernetwork_class`**

The implementation uses a deferred class-building pattern to satisfy INFRA-05 (CPU-only importability):

**Pattern Flow:**
1. `DocToLoraHypernetwork` is a module-level `_LazyHypernetworkProxy` instance (line 231)
2. When first instantiated, `_LazyHypernetworkProxy.__call__()` triggers `_build_hypernetwork_class()` (line 222)
3. `_build_hypernetwork_class()` imports torch/nn only then (line 41-42) and returns a real `nn.Module` subclass
4. All subsequent instantiations reuse the cached class via `_real_class`

This keeps the module importable without torch at module level.

### 3. **DocToLoraHypernetwork CLASS SIGNATURE**

Located within `_build_hypernetwork_class()` (lines 44-199), returns `_DocToLoraHypernetwork(nn.Module)`.

**`__init__` signature (lines 75-87):**
```python
def __init__(
    self,
    input_dim: int,
    num_latents: int = 32,
    latent_dim: int = 256,
    depth: int = 4,
    heads: int = 8,
    rank: int = 8,
    target_modules: Sequence[str] = ("q_proj", "v_proj"),
    num_layers: int = 28,
    hidden_dim: int = 4096,
    module_dims: dict[str, tuple[int, int]] | None = None,
) -> None:
```

**All attributes initialized (lines 88-103):**
- `self.rank: int = rank` (default: 8)
- `self.hidden_dim: int = hidden_dim` (default: 4096)
- `self.target_modules: list[str]` (default: ["q_proj", "v_proj"])
- `self.num_layers: int = num_layers` (default: 28)
- `self.num_latents: int = num_latents` (default: 32)
- `self.latent_dim: int = latent_dim` (default: 256)
- `self.module_dims: dict[str, tuple[int, int]]` — maps module name to (in_features, out_features)

### 4. **MODULE ATTRIBUTES (nn.Module submodules)**

**Exact initialization (lines 106-129):**

1. **`self.token_embedding`** (line 106):
   ```python
   nn.Embedding(input_dim, latent_dim)
   ```
   - Hypernetwork's own lightweight embedding (NOT the 7B model's embedding)
   - Input: vocab indices, output: latent_dim embeddings

2. **`self.latents`** (line 109):
   ```python
   nn.Parameter(torch.randn(num_latents, latent_dim))
   ```
   - Learnable parameter tensor
   - **FIXED SHAPE: (32, 256)** — tests confirm this is always (num_latents=32, latent_dim=256) by design
   - Unexpanded shape; expanded in forward() per batch

3. **`self.cross_attend`** (lines 112-114):
   ```python
   nn.MultiheadAttention(latent_dim, heads, batch_first=True)
   ```
   - Latents (Q) cross-attend over token embeddings (K, V)
   - Input: latent_dim=256, heads=8

4. **`self.self_attend`** (lines 117-120):
   ```python
   encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(latent_dim, heads, batch_first=True)
   nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=depth)
   ```
   - Self-attention stack over latents
   - depth=4 layers by default
   - Each layer: latent_dim=256, heads=8

5. **`self.weight_head`** (line 129):
   ```python
   nn.Linear(latent_dim * num_latents, total_params)
   ```
   - Input: flattened latents (32 * 256 = 8192)
   - Output: all LoRA weight parameters

**Total output params calculation (lines 124-128):**
```python
for m in target_modules:
    in_f, out_f = self.module_dims[m]
    total_params += num_layers * (rank * in_f + out_f * rank)
```
- For each module: `num_layers * (rank * in_features + out_features * rank)`
- With defaults (rank=8, hidden_dim=4096, num_layers=28, 2 modules):
  - per-module per-layer: 8 * 4096 + 4096 * 8 = 65,536 params
  - total: 2 modules * 28 layers * 65,536 = 3,670,016 params

### 5. **FORWARD METHOD SIGNATURE AND BEHAVIOR**

**Signature (lines 131-142):**
```python
def forward(self, token_ids: torch.Tensor) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
```

**Input:**
- `token_ids`: shape `(batch_size, seq_len)`, dtype `torch.long`

**Processing steps (lines 143-167):**

1. **Token embedding** (line 146):
   ```python
   x = self.token_embedding(token_ids)  # (batch, seq_len, latent_dim)
   ```

2. **Latent expansion** (lines 149-151):
   ```python
   latents = self.latents.unsqueeze(0).expand(batch_size, -1, -1)
   # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   ```

3. **Cross-attention** (lines 154-156):
   ```python
   latents, _ = self.cross_attend(latents, x, x)
   # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   ```
   - Latents are Q, embeddings are K and V

4. **Self-attention stack** (line 159):
   ```python
   latents = self.self_attend(latents)  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   ```

5. **Flatten** (lines 162-164):
   ```python
   latents = latents.reshape(batch_size, -1)
   # (batch, num_latents * latent_dim)
   ```

6. **Linear head** (line 167):
   ```python
   weights = self.weight_head(latents)  # (batch, total_weight_params)
   ```

**Output format (lines 169-199):**
- Returns dict of PEFT state_dict keys → tensors
- **Always processes first batch element** (index 0)
- **Key naming pattern:**
  ```
  base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_{A|B}.weight
  ```
- **Tensor shapes:**
  - `lora_A`: `(rank, in_features)` — line 184-186
  - `lora_B`: `(out_features, rank)` — line 194-196

### 6. **DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE CONSTANT**

- **Definition:** line 29
- **Value:** `32000`
- **Usage:** Default for tokenization when vocab_size not specified

### 7. **TRAJECTORY_TO_TOKENS FUNCTION**

**Signature (lines 266-270):**
```python
def trajectory_to_tokens(
    trajectory_text: str,
    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
    max_length: int = 2048,
) -> "torch.Tensor":
```

**Algorithm (lines 288-300):**
1. Extract character trigrams from trajectory_text (lines 289-292)
2. Hash each trigram: `hash(trigram) % vocab_size` → token ID
3. Pad or truncate to max_length (lines 295-298)
4. Return shape `(1, max_length)` as torch.long tensor (line 300)

**Returns:** `torch.tensor([tokens], dtype=torch.long)` — batch size 1

### 8. **SAVE_HYPERNETWORK_ADAPTER FUNCTION**

**Signature (lines 351-357):**
```python
def save_hypernetwork_adapter(
    weights: dict[str, "torch.Tensor"],
    output_dir: str,
    base_model_id: str,
    rank: int = 8,
    target_modules: list[str] | None = None,
) -> None:
```

**Implementation (lines 380-403):**

1. **Create output directory** (line 381):
   ```python
   output_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   ```

2. **Write safetensors file** (lines 384-385):
   ```python
   safetensors_path = output_path / "adapter_model.safetensors"
   save_file(weights, str(safetensors_path))
   ```

3. **Write adapter_config.json** (lines 388-403):
   - Uses safetensors.torch.save_file (line 375)
   - Config dict structure (lines 388-400):
     ```python
     {
         "peft_type": "LORA",
         "r": rank,
         "lora_alpha": rank * 2,
         "target_modules": target_modules,
         "lora_dropout": 0.0,
         "bias": "none",
         "task_type": "CAUSAL_LM",
         "base_model_name_or_path": base_model_id,
         "inference_mode": True,
         "modules_to_save": None,  # CRITICAL: None to avoid vLLM rejection
         "fan_in_fan_out": False,
     }
     ```
   - **CRITICAL DECISION (Phase 21-01):** `modules_to_save` is explicitly `None` — NOT array of modules (comment line 372-373)

### 9. **GENERATE_ADAPTER FUNCTION**

**Signature (lines 303-311):**
```python
def generate_adapter(
    hypernetwork: Any,
    trajectory_text: str,
    output_dir: str,
    base_model_id: str,
    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
    max_length: int = 2048,
    device: str = "cpu",
) -> str:
```

**Implementation (lines 312-348):**
1. Infer vocab size from hypernetwork if available (lines 329-332)
2. Call `trajectory_to_tokens()` (line 333)
3. Move tensors to device (line 334)
4. Forward pass with `torch.no_grad()` (lines 336-337)
5. Extract rank and target_modules from hypernetwork (lines 339-340)
6. Call `save_hypernetwork_adapter()` (lines 341-347)
7. Return output_dir (line 348)

### 10. **LOAD_PRETRAINED FUNCTION**

**Signature (lines 234-238):**
```python
def load_pretrained(
    checkpoint_path: str,
    device: str = "cpu",
    **kwargs: Any,
) -> Any:
```

**Implementation (lines 234-263):**
- Loads checkpoint with `torch.load(..., weights_only=True)`
- Extracts `hypernetwork_config` from checkpoint
- Instantiates DocToLoraHypernetwork with config
- Loads state_dict
- Returns on specified device in eval mode

### 11. **SUPPORTING FILES**

#### `/libs/model-training/src/model_training/config.py`
- `get_training_config(task_type, rank=64, epochs=3, learning_rate=2e-4)` → dict
- `validate_config(config)` → bool or raises ValueError
- Target modules: `["q_proj", "v_proj"]`

#### `/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trainer.py`
- `train_qlora(session_id, adapter_id, output_dir, ...)` — QLoRA gradient-descent training
  - Uses SFTTrainer, NF4 quantization, bfloat16
  - All GPU imports deferred inside function
  - Reads RUNE_BASE_MODEL env var (default: `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct`)
- `train_and_register(...)` — combines train_qlora + AdapterRegistry.store()

#### `/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py`
- `record_trajectory(session_id, steps, outcome, ...)` → dict with session_id, file_path
- `load_trajectory(trajectory_id)` → dict or FileNotFoundError
- `format_for_sft(trajectory)` → list[dict[role, content]] or []
  - Only succeeds if outcome == "success"

#### `/libs/model-training/src/model_training/sakana_d2l.py`
- Integration with Sakana's `ctx-to-lora` (from https://github.com/SakanaAI/doc-to-lora.git)
- `load_sakana_checkpoint()` — loads HyperLoRA perceiver with flash attention patches
- `extract_activations()` — per-layer hidden states from base model
- `generate_adapter_from_sakana()` — end-to-end text → adapters
- `_save_sakana_adapter()` — converts Sakana format to PEFT flat state_dict

#### `/libs/model-training/src/model_training/peft_utils.py`
- `build_qlora_config(rank, alpha, target_modules, dropout=0.1)` → LoraConfig
- `apply_lora_adapter(model, config)` → PEFT-wrapped model
- `merge_adapter(model)` → raises NotImplementedError

### 12. **DEPENDENCIES**

**From pyproject.toml (line 64):**
```toml
[tool.uv.sources]
ctx-to-lora = { git = "https://github.com/SakanaAI/doc-to-lora.git" }
```

**Optional GPU dependencies (lines 13-21 in model-training/pyproject.toml):**
- safetensors>=0.4.0
- torch>=2.0.0
- peft>=0.18.0
- bitsandbytes>=0.45.0

### 14. **KEY DIMENSIONS SUMMARY**

| Parameter | Default | Notes |
|-----------|---------|-------|

| num_latents | 32 | FIXED for tests |
| latent_dim | 256 | FIXED for tests |
| depth | 4 | Self-attention stack layers |
| heads | 8 | Attention heads |
| rank | 8 | LoRA rank for generated adapters |
| target_modules | ["q_proj", "v_proj"] | Default PEFT targets |
| num_layers | 28 | Target model layers (for Qwen2.5-7B) |
| hidden_dim | 4096 | Target model hidden dim |
| module_dims | None | Override per-module dims (e.g., GQA KV cache) |

### 15. **CRITICAL PEFT STATE_DICT KEY FORMAT**

All generated weights use this exact pattern:
```
base_model.model.model.layers.{layer_idx}.self_attn.{module_name}.lora_{A|B}.weight
```

Examples:
- `base_model.model.model.layers.0.self_attn.q_proj.lora_A.weight`
- `base_model.model.model.layers.0.self_attn.q_proj.lora_B.weight`
- `base_model.model.model.layers.27.self_attn.v_proj.lora_A.weight`

### 16. **INFRA-05 COMPLIANCE**

All GPU imports are deferred inside function/method bodies:
- `hypernetwork.py`: torch imports only in `_build_hypernetwork_class()`, `load_pretrained()`, `trajectory_to_tokens()`, `generate_adapter()`, `save_hypernetwork_adapter()`
- `trainer.py`: All imports inside `train_qlora()`, `train_and_register()`
- `trajectory.py`: No GPU imports at all
- `config.py`: No GPU imports at all
- `peft_utils.py`: peft imports only inside functions

This keeps all modules importable in CPU-only CI environments.

**Summary:** The hypernetwork implementation is a Perceiver-based instant LoRA adapter generator that produces rank-8 LoRA weights in <1s without loading the 7B base model, using learned latents attending over token embeddings extracted from trajectory text. It generates PEFT-compatible adapters with exact state_dict keys for Qwen2.5-7B's 28 transformer layers.

**HardwareBudget Computation:**

- `training_slots = 1 if free_vram >= MIN_TRAINING_VRAM_MB else 0`

### 7. HYPERNETWORK ARCHITECTURE (`libs/model-training/src/model_training/hypernetwork.py`)

**DocToLoraHypernetwork (Custom Implementation):**

Default Parameters:
```python
DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE: int = 32000

class DocToLoraHypernetwork:
    input_dim: int                          # Vocab size for token embedding
    num_latents: int = 32                   # Learnable latent vectors
    latent_dim: int = 256                   # Latent dimensionality
    depth: int = 4                          # Self-attention layers
    heads: int = 8                          # Attention heads
    rank: int = 8                           # LoRA rank for generated adapters
    target_modules: tuple = ("q_proj", "v_proj")
    num_layers: int = 28                    # Transformer layers in base model
    hidden_dim: int = 4096                  # Default hidden dimension
    module_dims: dict | None                # Per-module dims for GQA support
```

**Architecture:**
1. Token embedding (hypernetwork's own, NOT base model's)
2. Learnable latent array: `(num_latents, latent_dim)`
3. Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
4. Self-attention stack on latents
5. Linear head: project to all LoRA weight parameters

**Key Functions:**
- `load_pretrained(checkpoint_path, device)` - Load from checkpoint
- `trajectory_to_tokens(text, vocab_size, max_length)` - Hash-based tokenization
- `generate_adapter(hypernetwork, trajectory_text, output_dir, base_model_id)`
- `save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, output_dir, base_model_id, rank, target_modules)`

**Note:** This is a separate, custom implementation from Sakana's `ctx-to-lora`. The custom one uses simple hash-based tokenization, while Sakana's uses more sophisticated perceiver architecture.

### 12. RUNE ARCHITECTURE SUMMARY

**Three Interacting Components:**

1. **Recursive Code Generation Loop**
   - Bootstrap → Generate → Execute → Reflect
   - Trajectory → Hypernetwork → Adapter
   - Compose adapter into library

2. **Adapter Hierarchy**
   - Project root adapter
   - Domain adapters (async, FastAPI, etc.)
   - Task-specific adapters
   - Evolution Operator manages lifecycle

3. **Serving Architecture**
   - vLLM server with dynamic LoRA adapter loading
   - Adapter registry (SQLite metadata)
   - S-LoRA unified paging for concurrent adapter serving

**Key Innovation:** LoRA weight space as persistent episodic memory across sessions.

## Comprehensive Exploration Report: Rune Monorepo Training Service & Test Patterns

Based on my thorough exploration of the rune monorepo, here is a complete analysis of the training service architecture, test patterns, mock strategies, and reusable code:

---

### 1. TRAINING SERVICE STRUCTURE

#### Main Entry Point: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/services/training-svc/src/training_svc/routers/training.py`

**Key Functions:**

- `_run_hypernetwork_job(job_id, adapter_id, trajectory_id, task_type)`: Background worker that:
  - Takes 4 arguments (as documented in memory)
  - Loads trajectory via `load_trajectory(trajectory_id)`
  - Formats messages via `format_for_sft(trajectory)` 
  - Tokenizes text using `AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(base_model_id)`
  - Instantiates `DocToLoraHypernetwork(input_dim=tokenizer.vocab_size)`
  - Loads pretrained weights via `torch.load(hypernetwork_weights_path, map_location="cpu")`
  - Runs forward pass: `weights = hypernetwork(token_ids)` (with `torch.no_grad()`)
  - Saves adapter via `save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, adapter_dir, base_model_id)`
  - Registers in `AdapterRegistry` with metadata (source="hypernetwork", rank=8, session_id=trajectory_id)
  - Updates `JOB_STORE[job_id]` status to "completed" or "failed"

- `train_hypernetwork(request, background_tasks)`: FastAPI endpoint that:
  - Validates `trajectory_ids` is non-empty (raises 422 if empty)
  - Assigns `adapter_id` at request time (not at job completion)
  - Queues `_run_hypernetwork_job` as background task
  - Returns immediately with `{"job_id": ..., "status": "queued"}`

**Environment Variables Used:**
```python
RUNE_BASE_MODEL = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct" (default)
RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH = Path.home() / ".rune" / "hypernetwork.pt" (default)
RUNE_ADAPTER_DIR = optional, defaults to ~/.rune/adapters/
RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR = optional, set by tests via monkeypatch
```

**Thread Safety Pattern:**
- Uses `_JOB_STORE_LOCK = threading.Lock()` from `training_svc.jobs`
- All `JOB_STORE[job_id]` access wrapped with `with _JOB_STORE_LOCK:`

---

### 3. MOCK STRATEGIES & GPU DEPENDENCY INJECTION

#### Pattern: INFRA-05 (Deferred GPU Imports)
All torch, transformers, safetensors, trl imports are INSIDE function bodies, never at module level. This keeps modules importable in CPU-only CI.

#### Test Hypernetwork Mocking (`test_hypernetwork.py`):

1. **Importability test** (lines 20-34):
   ```python
   def test_hypernetwork_importable_without_gpu() -> None:
       from model_training.hypernetwork import DocToLoraHypernetwork
       assert callable(DocToLoraHypernetwork)
   ```

2. **Fake GPU Module Injection** (lines 41-80):
   ```python
   _FAKE_HN_MODULES = ["torch", "torch.nn", "safetensors", "safetensors.torch"]
   
   def _inject_fake_hn_modules() -> None:
       fake_torch = ModuleType("torch")
       fake_torch.Tensor = MagicMock()
       fake_torch.randn = MagicMock()
       # ... build fake_nn, fake_safetensors similarly
       sys.modules.setdefault("torch", fake_torch)
       sys.modules.setdefault("torch.nn", fake_nn)
       sys.modules.setdefault("safetensors", fake_safetensors)
       sys.modules.setdefault("safetensors.torch", fake_safetensors_torch)
   ```

3. **Torch Availability Detection** (lines 86-96):
   ```python
   try:
       import torch
       import torch.nn
       _TORCH_AVAILABLE = not isinstance(torch, MagicMock) and hasattr(torch.nn, "Module")
   except ImportError:
       _TORCH_AVAILABLE = False
   
   requires_torch = pytest.mark.skipif(
       not _TORCH_AVAILABLE, reason="torch not installed in this environment"
   )
   ```

4. **Safetensors Module Ensurer** (lines 184-193):
   ```python
   def _ensure_safetensors_module() -> None:
       if "safetensors" not in sys.modules:
           fake_st = ModuleType("safetensors")
           fake_st_torch = ModuleType("safetensors.torch")
           fake_st_torch.save_file = MagicMock()
           fake_st.torch = fake_st_torch
           sys.modules["safetensors"] = fake_st
           sys.modules["safetensors.torch"] = fake_st_torch
   ```

#### Test Trainer Mocking (`test_trainer.py`):

**Fake GPU Modules List** (lines 131-142):
```python
_FAKE_GPU_MODULES = [
    "datasets",
    "torch",
    "transformers",
    "transformers.AutoModelForCausalLM",

**Injection Pattern** (lines 145-177):
```python
def _inject_fake_gpu_modules() -> None:
    fake_torch = ModuleType("torch")
    fake_torch.bfloat16 = MagicMock()
    
    fake_datasets = ModuleType("datasets")
    fake_dataset_cls = MagicMock()
    fake_dataset_cls.from_list = staticmethod(lambda records: MagicMock())
    fake_datasets.Dataset = fake_dataset_cls
    
    fake_transformers = ModuleType("transformers")
    fake_transformers.AutoModelForCausalLM = MagicMock()
    fake_transformers.AutoTokenizer = MagicMock()
    fake_transformers.BitsAndBytesConfig = MagicMock()
    
    fake_trl = ModuleType("trl")
    fake_trl.SFTConfig = MagicMock()
    fake_trl.SFTTrainer = MagicMock()
    
    fake_peft = ModuleType("peft")
    fake_peft.LoraConfig = MagicMock()
    fake_peft.get_peft_model = MagicMock()
    
    sys.modules.setdefault("torch", fake_torch)
    # ... register all others
```

**Removal Pattern** (lines 179-187):
```python
def _remove_fake_gpu_modules() -> None:
    for mod in ("torch", "datasets", "transformers", "trl", "peft"):
        if mod in sys.modules and isinstance(sys.modules[mod], ModuleType):
            m = sys.modules[mod]
            if getattr(m, "__file__", "FAKE") is None:
                del sys.modules[mod]

**Full Integration Flow:**
1. Creates in-memory SQLite test database
2. Pre-populates `JOB_STORE[job_id] = JobStatus(job_id, status="queued", adapter_id)`
3. Injects sys.modules mocks:
   - `torch` with `torch.no_grad()`, `torch.load()` mocks
   - `transformers.AutoTokenizer` returning mock with `vocab_size=32000`
   - `model_training.hypernetwork` with **real** `save_hypernetwork_adapter` (not mocked!)
   - `model_training.trajectory` with mocked `load_trajectory` and `format_for_sft`
4. Calls `_run_hypernetwork_job(job_id, adapter_id, trajectory_id, task_type)`
5. Verifies:
   - `JOB_STORE[job_id].status == "completed"`
   - `AdapterRegistry.retrieve_by_id(adapter_id)` returns record with:
     - `source == "hypernetwork"`
     - `rank == 8`
     - `task_type == task_type`
     - `session_id == trajectory_id`
     - `file_hash` is 64-char SHA-256 hex
     - `file_size_bytes > 0`

def test_hypernetwork_forward_peft_keys() -> None:
    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(
        input_dim=1000, num_layers=2, hidden_dim=32, 
        num_latents=4, latent_dim=32
    )
    token_ids = torch.zeros(1, 8, dtype=torch.long)

    for i in range(2):
        for module in ("q_proj", "v_proj"):
            for ab in ("A", "B"):
                key = f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_{ab}.weight"

@requires_torch
def test_hypernetwork_forward_lora_shapes() -> None:
    rank = 8
    hidden_dim = 64
    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(
        input_dim=32000, num_layers=1, rank=rank, hidden_dim=hidden_dim
    )
    token_ids = torch.zeros(1, 8, dtype=torch.long)
    result = model(token_ids)
    
    for module in ("q_proj", "v_proj"):
        key_a = f"base_model.model.model.layers.0.self_attn.{module}.lora_A.weight"
        key_b = f"base_model.model.model.layers.0.self_attn.{module}.lora_B.weight"
        assert result[key_a].shape == (rank, hidden_dim)
        assert result[key_b].shape == (hidden_dim, rank)

def test_save_hypernetwork_adapter_config_fields() -> None:
    _ensure_safetensors_module()
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
        output_dir = Path(tmpdir)
        
        with patch("safetensors.torch.save_file"):
            save_hypernetwork_adapter(
                weights={},
                output_dir=str(output_dir),
                base_model_id="Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B",
                rank=8,
                target_modules=["q_proj", "v_proj"],
            )
        
        config = json.loads((output_dir / "adapter_config.json").read_text())
        assert config["peft_type"] == "LORA"
        assert config["r"] == 8
        assert config["target_modules"] == ["q_proj", "v_proj"]
        assert config["inference_mode"] is True
        assert config.get("modules_to_save") is None  # Critical: vLLM rejects this
        assert config["task_type"] == "CAUSAL_LM"
        assert config["base_model_name_or_path"] == "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B"

def _make_factory(cls: type[T], defaults: dict[str, Any]) -> Callable[..., T]:
    """Return a factory callable that creates *cls* instances with merged defaults."""
    def _factory(**kwargs: Any) -> T:
        return cls(**{**defaults, **kwargs})
    return _factory

### 9. KEY HYPERNETWORK IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS

#### Lazy Proxy Pattern (`hypernetwork.py` lines 210-231):
```python
class _LazyHypernetworkProxy:
    """Lazy proxy for DocToLoraHypernetwork.
    
    Acts as a callable that creates a real nn.Module instance on first call.
    The class is rebuilt (and cached) on first instantiation so that torch is
    only imported when actually needed (INFRA-05 pattern).
    """
    _real_class: type | None = None
    
    def __call__(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        if self._real_class is None:
            self.__class__._real_class = _build_hypernetwork_class()
        return self._real_class(*args, **kwargs)
    
    def __instancecheck__(self, instance: object) -> bool:
        if self._real_class is None:
            return False
        return isinstance(instance, self._real_class)

DocToLoraHypernetwork: _LazyHypernetworkProxy = _LazyHypernetworkProxy()
```

#### Constructor Args:
```python
def __init__(
    self,
    input_dim: int,  # vocab_size from tokenizer
    num_latents: int = 32,
    latent_dim: int = 256,
    depth: int = 4,
    heads: int = 8,
    rank: int = 8,  # Fixed to 8 for hypernetwork
    target_modules: Sequence[str] = ("q_proj", "v_proj"),
    num_layers: int = 28,  # Target model (Qwen2.5-7B)
    hidden_dim: int = 4096,  # Target model hidden dim
    module_dims: dict[str, tuple[int, int]] | None = None,  # For GQA
) -> None:
```

#### forward() Signature:
```python
def forward(self, token_ids: torch.Tensor) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
    """Generate PEFT-compatible LoRA adapter weights from token IDs.
    
    Args:
        token_ids: Integer token ID tensor of shape (batch, seq_len).
    
    Returns:
        Dict mapping PEFT state_dict keys to weight tensors.
        Keys follow:
          base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_{A|B}.weight
        Returns first batch element (one adapter per trajectory).
    """
```

---

### 12. CRUCIAL DESIGN DECISIONS

1. **Hypernetwork rank is fixed to 8** - not configurable per adapter
2. **adapter_id is assigned at request time**, not at job completion (line 276 in training.py)
3. **No modules_to_save in LoraConfig** - vLLM rejects this (per Phase 21-01 decision)
4. **embed_tokens and lm_head excluded** from target_modules to prevent vLLM conflicts
5. **Token IDs come from base model's tokenizer**, not custom tokenization - hypernetwork learns its own embedding
6. **JOB_STORE is in-memory dict**, not database - status only tracked in RAM during job execution
7. **All GPU imports deferred to function bodies** - module-level imports are stdlib only
8. **Adapter files stored with sha256 hash** of safetensors file for integrity tracking

    54→    idefics2_mod.IDEFICS2_PERCEIVER_ATTENTION_CLASSES["flash_attention_2"] = (

    60→
    61→    def _patched_attn_fwd(
    62→        self: Any, *args: Any, is_cross_attn: Any = None, **kwargs: Any
    63→    ) -> Any:

    73→    def _eager_resampler_forward(

    75→        context: torch.Tensor,

    78→    ) -> torch.Tensor:

    81→        else:

    83→        latents = self.latents_q.unsqueeze(0).expand((bsz, *self.latents_q.size()))

   160→    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download  # noqa: PLC0415
   161→
   162→    logger.info("Downloading Sakana checkpoint %s from %s...", variant, HF_REPO_ID)
   163→    downloaded = Path(hf_hub_download(repo_id=HF_REPO_ID, filename=hf_filename))
   164→
   165→    cached.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   166→    import shutil  # noqa: PLC0415
   167→
   168→    shutil.copy2(downloaded, cached)
   169→    logger.info("Cached to: %s", cached)
   170→    return cached
   171→
   172→
   173→def load_sakana_checkpoint(
   174→    checkpoint_path: str | Path | None = None,
   175→    variant: str = DEFAULT_VARIANT,
   176→    device: str = "cpu",
   177→) -> tuple[Any, Any]:
   178→    """Load Sakana's HyperLoRA perceiver from checkpoint.
   179→
   180→    Downloads from HuggingFace if no local path is provided.
   181→    Patches flash attention for CPU/MPS compatibility.
   182→
   183→    Args:
   184→        checkpoint_path: Path to local checkpoint. If None, downloads from HF.
   185→        variant: HF checkpoint variant (only used if checkpoint_path is None).
   186→        device: Device to load onto.
   187→
   188→    Returns:
   189→        Tuple of (hypernet, hypernet_config).
   190→    """
   191→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   192→
   193→    _patch_flash_attention()
   194→
   195→    if checkpoint_path is None:
   196→        checkpoint_path = download_checkpoint(variant)
   197→
   198→    logger.info("Loading Sakana checkpoint: %s", checkpoint_path)
   199→    sd = torch.load(str(checkpoint_path), map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
   200→
   201→    hc = sd["hypernet_config"]
   202→    logger.info(
   203→        "HypernetConfig: latent_size=%d, lora_r=%d, base_model=%s",
   204→        hc.latent_size,
   205→        hc.lora_config.r,
   206→        sd["base_model_name_or_path"],
   207→    )
   208→
   209→    from ctx_to_lora.modeling.hypernet import HyperLoRA  # noqa: PLC0415
   210→
   211→    hypernet = HyperLoRA(hc).to(torch.float32)
   212→
   213→    # Load perceiver/aggregator/head weights from checkpoint
   214→    hypernet_keys = [
   215→        k for k in sd.keys() if k.startswith(("aggregator.", "head.", "extra_"))
   216→    ]
   217→    hypernet_sd = {k: sd[k] for k in hypernet_keys if k in hypernet.state_dict()}
   218→
   219→    if hypernet_sd:
   220→        loaded = hypernet.load_state_dict(hypernet_sd, strict=False)
   221→        logger.info("Loaded %d perceiver weight tensors", len(hypernet_sd))
   222→        if loaded.missing_keys:
   223→            logger.info("Missing keys: %d", len(loaded.missing_keys))
   224→    else:
   225→        full_state = {k: v for k, v in sd.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}
   226→        loaded = hypernet.load_state_dict(full_state, strict=False)
   227→        logger.info(
   228→            "Loaded full state: missing=%d, unexpected=%d",
   229→            len(loaded.missing_keys),
   230→            len(loaded.unexpected_keys),
   231→        )
   232→
   233→    hypernet = hypernet.to(device)
   234→    hypernet.eval()
   235→
   236→    param_count = sum(p.numel() for p in hypernet.parameters())
   237→    logger.info("HyperLoRA params: %d", param_count)
   238→
   239→    return hypernet, hc
   240→
   241→
   242→def extract_activations(
   243→    text: str,
   244→    base_model_name: str,
   245→    layer_indices: list[int],
   246→    device: str = "cpu",
   247→    max_length: int = 512,
   248→) -> tuple[Any, Any]:
   249→    """Extract per-layer hidden state activations from the base model.
   250→
   251→    Runs the input text through the base model and captures hidden states
   252→    at the specified layer indices. These activations are what Sakana's
   253→    HyperLoRA perceiver expects as input.
   254→
   255→    Args:
   256→        text: Input text to process.
   257→        base_model_name: HuggingFace model ID for the base model.
   258→        layer_indices: Which layers to extract activations from.
   259→        device: Device for computation.
   260→        max_length: Max token sequence length.
   261→
   262→    Returns:
   263→        Tuple of (features, attention_mask) ready for HyperLoRA.
   264→        features shape: (1, num_layers, seq_len, hidden_dim)
   265→        attention_mask shape: (1, seq_len)
   266→    """
   267→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   268→    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer  # noqa: PLC0415
   269→
   270→    logger.info("Loading base model %s for activation extraction...", base_model_name)
   271→    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(base_model_name)
   272→    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
   273→        base_model_name,
   274→        output_hidden_states=True,
   275→        torch_dtype=torch.float32,
   276→    )
   277→    model.to(device)
   278→    model.eval()
   279→
   280→    inputs = tokenizer(
   281→        text, return_tensors="pt", truncation=True, max_length=max_length
   282→    )
   283→    inputs = {k: v.to(device) for k, v in inputs.items()}
   284→
   285→    with torch.no_grad():
   286→        outputs = model(**inputs)
   287→
   288→    # outputs.hidden_states is a tuple of (num_layers+1,) tensors
   289→    # each of shape (batch, seq_len, hidden_dim)
   290→    hidden_states = outputs.hidden_states
   291→
   292→    # Stack selected layers: (batch, num_layers, seq_len, hidden_dim)
   293→    selected = torch.stack([hidden_states[i] for i in layer_indices], dim=1)
   294→    attention_mask = inputs["attention_mask"]
   295→
   296→    logger.info(
   297→        "Extracted activations: %s from %d layers", selected.shape, len(layer_indices)
   298→    )
   299→
   300→    # Free base model memory
   301→    del model
   302→    if device != "cpu":
   303→        torch.cuda.empty_cache() if "cuda" in device else None
   304→
   305→    return selected, attention_mask
   306→
   307→
   308→def generate_adapter_from_sakana(
   309→    text: str,
   310→    output_dir: str,
   311→    checkpoint_path: str | Path | None = None,
   312→    variant: str = DEFAULT_VARIANT,
   313→    base_model_name: str | None = None,
   314→    device: str = "cpu",
   315→) -> str:
   316→    """End-to-end: text → base model activations → HyperLoRA → PEFT adapter.
   317→
   318→    This is the main entry point. It:
   319→      1. Loads the Sakana pretrained perceiver (downloading if needed)
   320→      2. Runs text through the base model to get per-layer activations
   321→      3. Feeds activations through the perceiver to generate LoRA weights
   322→      4. Saves weights in PEFT-compatible format
   323→
   324→    Args:
   325→        text: Input text (trajectory, document, context) to encode.
   326→        output_dir: Directory to save the PEFT adapter files.
   327→        checkpoint_path: Path to local checkpoint, or None to download.
   328→        variant: HF checkpoint variant if downloading.
   329→        base_model_name: Override base model. If None, uses the one from checkpoint.
   330→        device: Device for computation.
   331→
   332→    Returns:
   333→        Path to the saved adapter directory.
   334→    """
   335→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   336→
   337→    hypernet, hc = load_sakana_checkpoint(checkpoint_path, variant, device)
   338→
   339→    # Determine base model from checkpoint config
   340→    if base_model_name is None:
   341→        # Load checkpoint just to read base_model_name_or_path
   342→        if checkpoint_path is None:
   343→            checkpoint_path = download_checkpoint(variant)
   344→        sd = torch.load(str(checkpoint_path), map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
   345→        base_model_name = sd["base_model_name_or_path"]
   346→        del sd
   347→
   348→    logger.info("Base model: %s", base_model_name)
   349→
   350→    # Extract activations from base model
   351→    layer_indices = list(hc.layer_indices)
   352→    features, attn_mask = extract_activations(
   353→        text=text,
   354→        base_model_name=base_model_name,
   355→        layer_indices=layer_indices,
   356→        device=device,
   357→        max_length=512,
   358→    )
   359→
   360→    # Generate LoRA weights via perceiver
   361→    logger.info("Generating LoRA weights via HyperLoRA perceiver...")
   362→    with torch.no_grad():
   363→        lora_dict, layernorm_dict = hypernet.generate_weights(
   364→            features, attn_mask, None
   365→        )
   366→
   367→    # Save as PEFT adapter
   368→    _save_sakana_adapter(
   369→        lora_dict=lora_dict,
   370→        output_dir=output_dir,
   371→        base_model_name=base_model_name,
   372→        hc=hc,
   373→    )
   374→
   375→    return output_dir
   376→
   377→
   378→def _save_sakana_adapter(
   379→    lora_dict: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   380→    output_dir: str,
   381→    base_model_name: str,
   382→    hc: Any,
   383→) -> None:
   384→    """Save Sakana's HyperLoRA output as a PEFT-compatible adapter.
   385→
   386→    Converts from Sakana's format (dict of module → {A, B} with batch/layer dims)
   387→    to PEFT's flat state_dict format.
   388→
   389→    Args:
   390→        lora_dict: Output from HyperLoRA.generate_weights().
   391→        output_dir: Directory to write adapter files.
   392→        base_model_name: Base model identifier for adapter config.
   393→        hc: HypernetConfig with lora rank and target modules.
   394→    """
   395→    from safetensors.torch import save_file  # noqa: PLC0415
   396→
   397→    output_path = Path(output_dir)
   398→    output_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   399→
   400→    layer_indices = list(hc.layer_indices)
   401→    target_modules = list(hc.lora_config.target_modules)
   402→    rank = hc.lora_config.r
   403→
   404→    # Convert Sakana format → PEFT flat state_dict
   405→    # Sakana: lora_dict[module_name]["A"] shape (batch, num_layers, rank, in_features)
   406→    # PEFT: base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.mlp.{module}.lora_A.weight (rank, in_features)
   407→    state_dict = {}
   408→    for mod_name, weights in lora_dict.items():
   409→        if mod_name not in target_modules:
   410→            continue
   411→        a_weights = weights["A"]  # (batch, num_layers, rank, in_features)
   412→        b_weights = weights["B"]  # (batch, num_layers, rank, out_features)
   413→
   414→        for layer_pos, layer_idx in enumerate(layer_indices):
   415→            key_a = (
   416→                f"base_model.model.model.layers.{layer_idx}"
   417→                f".mlp.{mod_name}.lora_A.weight"
   418→            )
   419→            key_b = (
   420→                f"base_model.model.model.layers.{layer_idx}"
   421→                f".mlp.{mod_name}.lora_B.weight"
   422→            )
   423→            # Take first batch element, transpose B to (out_features, rank)
   424→            state_dict[key_a] = a_weights[0, layer_pos].contiguous()
   425→            state_dict[key_b] = b_weights[0, layer_pos].t().contiguous()
   426→
   427→    save_file(state_dict, str(output_path / "adapter_model.safetensors"))
   428→
   429→    adapter_config = {
   430→        "peft_type": "LORA",
   431→        "r": rank,
   432→        "lora_alpha": rank * 2,
   433→        "target_modules": target_modules,
   434→        "lora_dropout": 0.0,
   435→        "bias": "none",
   436→        "task_type": "CAUSAL_LM",
   437→        "base_model_name_or_path": base_model_name,
   438→        "inference_mode": True,
   439→        "modules_to_save": None,
   440→        "fan_in_fan_out": False,
   441→    }
   442→    (output_path / "adapter_config.json").write_text(json.dumps(adapter_config, indent=2))
   443→
   444→    logger.info(
   445→        "Saved PEFT adapter: %d tensors, %d layers, rank=%d, targets=%s",
   446→        len(state_dict),
   447→        len(layer_indices),
   448→        rank,
   449→        target_modules,
   450→    )
   451→
```

     1→"""DocToLoraHypernetwork: Perceiver-based instant LoRA adapter generation.
     2→
     3→Generates rank-8 LoRA adapter weights from token IDs in a single forward pass.
     4→Distinct from the QLoRA gradient-descent path (Phase 21) — this produces adapters
     5→in <1s by cross-attending over token embeddings with learned latents.
     6→
     7→IMPORTANT: All GPU imports (torch, safetensors) are deferred inside function/method
     8→bodies per INFRA-05 pattern — this module is importable in CPU-only CI.
     9→
    10→Usage:
    11→    from model_training.hypernetwork import (
    12→        DocToLoraHypernetwork,
    13→        save_hypernetwork_adapter,
    14→    )
    15→
    16→    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(input_dim=DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE)
    17→    weights = model(token_ids)
    18→    save_hypernetwork_adapter(weights, "/tmp/adapter", "Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B")
    19→"""
    20→
    21→from __future__ import annotations
    22→
    23→import json
    24→from pathlib import Path
    25→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Sequence
    26→
    27→# Default vocabulary size for the hypernetwork input embedding.
    28→# Used when the tokenizer vocabulary size is not explicitly provided.
    29→DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE: int = 32000
    30→
    31→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    32→    import torch
    33→
    34→
    35→def _build_hypernetwork_class() -> type:
    36→    """Build and return DocToLoraHypernetwork as a real nn.Module subclass.
    37→
    38→    This function is called the first time DocToLoraHypernetwork is instantiated
    39→    so that torch.nn.Module is only imported when needed (INFRA-05 pattern).
    40→    """
    41→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
    42→    import torch.nn as nn  # noqa: PLC0415
    43→
    44→    class _DocToLoraHypernetwork(nn.Module):
    45→        """Perceiver-based hypernetwork that generates LoRA adapter weights.
    46→
    47→        Given a sequence of token IDs (from a document/trajectory), produces a
    48→        PEFT-compatible state_dict with lora_A and lora_B matrices for every
    49→        target_module across all transformer layers — in a single forward pass.
    50→
    51→        Architecture:
    52→        - Lightweight learned token embedding (not the 7B base model's embedding)
    53→        - Learnable latent array (num_latents x latent_dim)
    54→        - Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
    55→        - Self-attention stack: process latents
    56→        - Linear head: project to all LoRA weight parameters
    57→
    58→        Args:
    59→            input_dim: Vocabulary size for the hypernetwork's own token embedding.
    60→            num_latents: Number of learnable latent vectors. Default: 32.
    61→            latent_dim: Dimensionality of each latent vector. Default: 256.
    62→            depth: Number of self-attention layers over latents. Default: 4.
    63→            heads: Number of attention heads. Default: 8.
    64→            rank: LoRA rank for generated adapters. Default: 8.
    65→            target_modules: LoRA target module names. Default: ("q_proj", "v_proj").
    66→            num_layers: Number of transformer layers in target model. Default: 28.
    67→            hidden_dim: Hidden dim of target model. Default: 4096. Used as
    68→                fallback when module_dims is not provided.
    69→            module_dims: Per-module output dimensions for GQA models where
    70→                q_proj and v_proj have different sizes. Maps module name to
    71→                (in_features, out_features). If None, all modules use
    72→                (hidden_dim, hidden_dim).
    73→        """
    74→
    75→        def __init__(
    76→            self,
    77→            input_dim: int,
    78→            num_latents: int = 32,
    79→            latent_dim: int = 256,
    80→            depth: int = 4,
    81→            heads: int = 8,
    82→            rank: int = 8,
    83→            target_modules: Sequence[str] = ("q_proj", "v_proj"),
    84→            num_layers: int = 28,
    85→            hidden_dim: int = 4096,
    86→            module_dims: dict[str, tuple[int, int]] | None = None,
    87→        ) -> None:
    88→            super().__init__()
    89→
    90→            self.rank = rank
    91→            self.hidden_dim = hidden_dim
    92→            self.target_modules: list[str] = list(target_modules)
    93→            self.num_layers = num_layers
    94→            self.num_latents = num_latents
    95→            self.latent_dim = latent_dim
    96→
    97→            # Per-module dimensions: (in_features, out_features)
    98→            # For GQA: q_proj is (hidden, hidden) but v_proj is (hidden, kv_dim)
    99→            if module_dims is not None:
   100→                self.module_dims = module_dims
   101→            else:
   102→                _default_dims = (hidden_dim, hidden_dim)
   103→                self.module_dims = dict.fromkeys(target_modules, _default_dims)
   104→
   105→            # Hypernetwork's own lightweight embedding — does NOT load the 7B model
   106→            self.token_embedding = nn.Embedding(input_dim, latent_dim)
   107→
   108→            # Learnable latent array: shape (num_latents, latent_dim)
   109→            self.latents = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(num_latents, latent_dim))
   110→
   111→            # Cross-attention: latents (Q) attend over token embeddings (K, V)
   112→            self.cross_attend = nn.MultiheadAttention(
   113→                latent_dim, heads, batch_first=True
   114→            )
   115→
   116→            # Self-attention stack to process latents
   117→            encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
   118→                latent_dim, heads, batch_first=True
   119→            )
   120→            self.self_attend = nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=depth)
   121→
   122→            # Project flattened latents to all LoRA weight parameters.
   123→            # Each (layer, module) needs lora_A and lora_B with per-module dims.
   124→            total_params = 0
   125→            for m in target_modules:
   126→                in_f, out_f = self.module_dims[m]
   127→                # lora_A: (rank, in_features), lora_B: (out_features, rank)
   128→                total_params += num_layers * (rank * in_f + out_f * rank)
   129→            self.weight_head = nn.Linear(latent_dim * num_latents, total_params)
   130→
   131→        def forward(self, token_ids: torch.Tensor) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
   132→            """Generate PEFT-compatible LoRA adapter weights from token IDs.
   133→
   134→            Args:
   135→                token_ids: Integer token ID tensor of shape (batch, seq_len).
   136→
   137→            Returns:
   138→                Dict mapping PEFT state_dict keys to weight tensors.
   139→                Keys follow:
   140→                  base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_{A|B}.weight
   141→                Returns first batch element (one adapter per trajectory).
   142→            """
   143→            batch_size = token_ids.shape[0]
   144→
   145→            # Embed token IDs with hypernetwork's own lightweight embedding
   146→            x = self.token_embedding(token_ids)  # (batch, seq_len, latent_dim)
   147→
   148→            # Expand latents for batch dimension
   149→            latents = self.latents.unsqueeze(0).expand(
   150→                batch_size, -1, -1
   151→            )  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   152→
   153→            # Cross-attention: latents attend over token embeddings
   154→            latents, _ = self.cross_attend(
   155→                latents, x, x
   156→            )  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   157→
   158→            # Self-attention stack over latents
   159→            latents = self.self_attend(latents)  # (batch, num_latents, latent_dim)
   160→
   161→            # Flatten latents
   162→            latents = latents.reshape(
   163→                batch_size, -1
   164→            )  # (batch, num_latents * latent_dim)
   165→
   166→            # Project to all LoRA weight parameters
   167→            weights = self.weight_head(latents)  # (batch, total_weight_params)
   168→
   169→            # Reshape into PEFT state_dict for first batch element (index 0)
   170→            state_dict: dict[str, torch.Tensor] = {}
   171→            offset = 0
   172→
   173→            for i in range(self.num_layers):
   174→                for module in self.target_modules:
   175→                    in_f, out_f = self.module_dims[module]
   176→                    a_size = self.rank * in_f
   177→                    b_size = out_f * self.rank
   178→
   179→                    # lora_A: shape (rank, in_features)
   180→                    key_a = (
   181→                        f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}"
   182→                        f".self_attn.{module}.lora_A.weight"
   183→                    )
   184→                    state_dict[key_a] = weights[0, offset : offset + a_size].reshape(
   185→                        self.rank, in_f
   186→                    )
   187→                    offset += a_size
   188→
   189→                    # lora_B: shape (out_features, rank)
   190→                    key_b = (
   191→                        f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}"
   192→                        f".self_attn.{module}.lora_B.weight"
   193→                    )
   194→                    state_dict[key_b] = weights[0, offset : offset + b_size].reshape(
   195→                        out_f, self.rank
   196→                    )
   197→                    offset += b_size
   198→
   199→            return state_dict
   200→
   201→    return _DocToLoraHypernetwork
   202→
   203→
   204→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   205→# DocToLoraHypernetwork: lazy proxy that builds the real nn.Module class on
   206→# first instantiation. This keeps the module importable without torch.
   207→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   208→
   209→
   210→class _LazyHypernetworkProxy:
   211→    """Lazy proxy for DocToLoraHypernetwork.
   212→
   213→    Acts as a callable that creates a real nn.Module instance on first call.
   214→    The class is rebuilt (and cached) on first instantiation so that torch is
   215→    only imported when actually needed (INFRA-05 pattern).
   216→    """
   217→
   218→    _real_class: type | None = None
   219→
   220→    def __call__(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
   221→        if self._real_class is None:
   222→            self.__class__._real_class = _build_hypernetwork_class()
   223→        return self._real_class(*args, **kwargs)  # type: ignore[misc]
   224→
   225→    def __instancecheck__(self, instance: object) -> bool:
   226→        if self._real_class is None:
   227→            return False
   228→        return isinstance(instance, self._real_class)
   229→
   230→
   231→DocToLoraHypernetwork: _LazyHypernetworkProxy = _LazyHypernetworkProxy()
   232→
   233→
   234→def load_pretrained(
   235→    checkpoint_path: str,
   236→    device: str = "cpu",
   237→    **kwargs: Any,
   238→) -> Any:
   239→    """Load a pretrained DocToLoraHypernetwork from a checkpoint.
   240→
   241→    Args:
   242→        checkpoint_path: Path to the .pt checkpoint file.
   243→        device: Device to load onto ('cpu', 'cuda', 'mps'). Default: 'cpu'.
   244→        **kwargs: Override constructor args (input_dim, num_latents, etc.).
   245→
   246→    Returns:
   247→        DocToLoraHypernetwork nn.Module loaded with pretrained weights.
   248→    """
   249→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   250→
   251→    checkpoint = torch.load(checkpoint_path, map_location=device, weights_only=True)
   252→
   253→    # Extract constructor args from checkpoint or use defaults/overrides
   254→    ctor_args = checkpoint.get("hypernetwork_config", {})
   255→    ctor_args.update(kwargs)
   256→    if "input_dim" not in ctor_args:
   257→        ctor_args["input_dim"] = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE
   258→
   259→    model = DocToLoraHypernetwork(**ctor_args)
   260→    model.load_state_dict(checkpoint["model_state_dict"])
   261→    model = model.to(device)
   262→    model.eval()
   263→    return model
   264→
   265→
   266→def trajectory_to_tokens(
   267→    trajectory_text: str,
   268→    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
   269→    max_length: int = 2048,
   270→) -> "torch.Tensor":
   271→    """Encode trajectory text as token IDs for the hypernetwork.
   272→
   273→    Uses a simple hash-based tokenization (character trigrams mapped to
   274→    vocab indices). This is intentionally simple — the hypernetwork learns
   275→    its own embedding, so exact tokenization doesn't matter as long as
   276→    it's consistent.
   277→
   278→    Args:
   279→        trajectory_text: Text to encode (plan, code diffs, test results, etc.).
   280→        vocab_size: Size of the hypernetwork's embedding vocabulary.
   281→        max_length: Maximum sequence length (truncates or pads).
   282→
   283→    Returns:
   284→        Token ID tensor of shape (1, max_length) ready for hypernetwork forward().
   285→    """
   286→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   287→
   288→    tokens: list[int] = []
   289→    for i in range(0, len(trajectory_text) - 2):
   290→        trigram = trajectory_text[i : i + 3]
   291→        token_id = hash(trigram) % vocab_size
   292→        tokens.append(token_id)
   293→
   294→    # Pad or truncate to max_length
   295→    if len(tokens) < max_length:
   296→        tokens.extend([0] * (max_length - len(tokens)))
   297→    else:
   298→        tokens = tokens[:max_length]
   299→
   300→    return torch.tensor([tokens], dtype=torch.long)
   301→
   302→
   303→def generate_adapter(
   304→    hypernetwork: Any,
   305→    trajectory_text: str,
   306→    output_dir: str,
   307→    base_model_id: str,
   308→    vocab_size: int = DEFAULT_VOCAB_SIZE,
   309→    max_length: int = 2048,
   310→    device: str = "cpu",
   311→) -> str:
   312→    """End-to-end: encode trajectory, run hypernetwork, save adapter.
   313→
   314→    Args:
   315→        hypernetwork: A DocToLoraHypernetwork instance.
   316→        trajectory_text: Text to encode into the adapter.
   317→        output_dir: Directory to save the adapter files.
   318→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID of the base model.
   319→        vocab_size: Vocabulary size for tokenization.
   320→        max_length: Max token sequence length.
   321→        device: Device for tensor operations.
   322→
   323→    Returns:
   324→        Path to the saved adapter directory.
   325→    """
   326→    import torch  # noqa: PLC0415
   327→
   328→    # Infer vocab size from hypernetwork's embedding if available
   329→    h_vocab = getattr(
   330→        getattr(hypernetwork, "token_embedding", None), "num_embeddings", None
   331→    )
   332→    effective_vocab = h_vocab if h_vocab is not None else vocab_size
   333→    tokens = trajectory_to_tokens(trajectory_text, effective_vocab, max_length)
   334→    tokens = tokens.to(device)
   335→
   336→    with torch.no_grad():
   337→        weights = hypernetwork(tokens)
   338→
   339→    rank = getattr(hypernetwork, "rank", 8)
   340→    target_mods = getattr(hypernetwork, "target_modules", ["q_proj", "v_proj"])
   341→    save_hypernetwork_adapter(
   342→        weights,
   343→        output_dir,
   344→        base_model_id,
   345→        rank=rank,
   346→        target_modules=target_mods,
   347→    )
   348→    return output_dir
   349→
   350→
   351→def save_hypernetwork_adapter(
   352→    weights: dict[str, "torch.Tensor"],
   353→    output_dir: str,
   354→    base_model_id: str,
   355→    rank: int = 8,
   356→    target_modules: list[str] | None = None,
   357→) -> None:
   358→    """Serialize hypernetwork-generated LoRA weights in PEFT adapter format.
   359→
   360→    Writes:
   361→    - adapter_model.safetensors: the LoRA weight tensors
   362→    - adapter_config.json: PEFT-compatible configuration
   363→
   364→    Args:
   365→        weights: PEFT state_dict from DocToLoraHypernetwork.forward().
   366→        output_dir: Directory to write adapter files to (created if needed).
   367→        base_model_id: HuggingFace model ID of the base model.
   368→        rank: LoRA rank. Default: 8.
   369→        target_modules: List of module names. Default: ["q_proj", "v_proj"].
   370→
   371→    Note:
   372→        Does NOT include embed_tokens or lm_head — vLLM rejects these in adapters
   373→        (per Phase 21-01 decision: no modules_to_save in LoraConfig).
   374→    """
   375→    from safetensors.torch import save_file  # noqa: PLC0415
   376→
   377→    if target_modules is None:
   378→        target_modules = ["q_proj", "v_proj"]
   379→
   380→    output_path = Path(output_dir)
   381→    output_path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   382→
   383→    # Write adapter weights as safetensors
   384→    safetensors_path = output_path / "adapter_model.safetensors"
   385→    save_file(weights, str(safetensors_path))
   386→
   387→    # Write PEFT-compatible adapter_config.json
   388→    adapter_config: dict[str, object] = {
   389→        "peft_type": "LORA",
   390→        "r": rank,
   391→        "lora_alpha": rank * 2,
   392→        "target_modules": target_modules,
   393→        "lora_dropout": 0.0,
   394→        "bias": "none",
   395→        "task_type": "CAUSAL_LM",
   396→        "base_model_name_or_path": base_model_id,
   397→        "inference_mode": True,
   398→        "modules_to_save": None,
   399→        "fan_in_fan_out": False,
   400→    }
   401→
   402→    config_path = output_path / "adapter_config.json"
   403→    config_path.write_text(json.dumps(adapter_config, indent=2))
   404→
```

## Comprehensive Revision Plan for `instructions/add_lora_training_code.md`

Below is every issue, verified against the actual codebase, with the specific revision needed.

---

### Issue 1: `Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next` does not exist

**Evidence:** Zero references in the codebase. No such model on HuggingFace. The codebase default is `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct` (used in `services/training-svc/src/training_svc/routers/training.py` line 163 and `services/rune-agent/src/rune_agent/nodes.py`).

**Revision:** Replace all occurrences of `Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next` with an explicit placeholder pattern: `<BASE_MODEL_ID>` with a note stating: *"Replace with the actual target model ID. The current codebase default is `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct`. If targeting a different Qwen variant, verify it exists on HuggingFace before proceeding."* Alternatively, if the intent is to stay with the current model, use `Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct` directly.

**Affected sections:** TARGET MODEL (line 18), CONFIG UPDATE (line 274), TRAINING SCRIPT (lines 189, 198), DELIVERABLES shell commands (lines 318-367), and the CONSTRAINTS "always read from cfg" pattern.

---

### Issue 2: Latent dimension mismatch (512 vs 256)

**Evidence:** `hypernetwork.py` line 79: `latent_dim: int = 256`. Test at `test_hypernetwork.py` line 115: `assert model.latents.shape == (32, 256)`. The instructions say "512-dim internal dimension" and `latent_dim: int = 512` (lines 50, 95, 278).

**Revision:** Change all references from `512` to `256` for `latent_dim`, OR explicitly acknowledge that this is a change from the current default and explain why it is being increased. The `modality_proj` line on instruction line 95 should read:
```
self.modality_proj = nn.Linear(hidden_size, latent_dim)  # 256
```
The CONFIG UPDATE section (line 278) should use `latent_dim: int = 256` to match the existing default. If the intent truly is to increase to 512, the instructions must explicitly say "change latent_dim from the current default of 256 to 512" and note that this breaks compatibility with existing checkpoints.

---

### Issue 3: Architecture confusion -- custom hypernetwork vs Sakana perceiver

**Evidence:**
- Custom `DocToLoraHypernetwork` (`hypernetwork.py` lines 112-120): one `nn.MultiheadAttention` for `cross_attend`, a `nn.TransformerEncoder` with `depth=4` layers for `self_attend`.
- Sakana `HyperLoRA` (`sakana_d2l.py` lines 209, 214-215): completely different architecture using `Idefics2Perceiver` with keys like `aggregator.*`, `head.*`.
- Instructions line 49: "8 cross-attention blocks (cross_attend in current code)" -- WRONG. There is 1 `nn.MultiheadAttention`.
- Instructions line 50: "512-dim internal dimension" -- WRONG. It is 256.
- Instructions line 53: "Perceiver learned a general 'document to latent representation' mapping" -- this conflates the Sakana perceiver (which does work this way) with the custom `DocToLoraHypernetwork` (which uses hash-based token IDs, not hidden states).

**Revision:** The CHECKPOINT STRATEGY section must be rewritten to acknowledge that `qwen_4b_d2l` is a **Sakana checkpoint** with completely different module names (`aggregator.*`, `head.*`, perceiver resampler layers) that are **architecturally incompatible** with `DocToLoraHypernetwork` (which has `token_embedding`, `cross_attend`, `self_attend`, `latents`, `weight_head`).

Two viable approaches should be presented:

**Option A -- Use Sakana's architecture directly:** Extend `sakana_d2l.py` (which already handles loading `qwen_4b_d2l`, extracting activations, and generating adapters) to support a new base model. This is 80% done. The training script would fine-tune the Sakana `HyperLoRA` perceiver, not `DocToLoraHypernetwork`.

**Option B -- Retrain the custom hypernetwork from scratch:** Modify `DocToLoraHypernetwork` to accept hidden states instead of token IDs (the modality_proj change), but do NOT attempt partial weight transfer from `qwen_4b_d2l` since the architectures are incompatible. Train from random init.

The instructions should NOT claim that partial weight transfer from `qwen_4b_d2l` into `DocToLoraHypernetwork` is possible. The "FROZEN" section listing "8 cross-attention blocks" must be removed entirely or corrected to: "1 `nn.MultiheadAttention` cross-attention layer and a `nn.TransformerEncoder` with `depth` self-attention layers."

---

### Issue 4: Env var name inconsistency

**Evidence:** Instructions line 156: `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_CHECKPOINT`. Codebase (`training_svc/routers/training.py` line 166, `test_training.py` line 140): `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH`.

**Revision:** Change `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_CHECKPOINT` to `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH` throughout the instructions (lines 156, 349, 351, 355, 362, 365) to match the existing convention.

---

### Issue 5: `extract_hidden_states` duplicates `extract_activations`

**Evidence:** `sakana_d2l.py` lines 242-305 already has `extract_activations()` which does exactly what the proposed `extract_hidden_states` helper does: text in, base model forward pass, hidden states out. It even handles model loading, tokenization, device placement, and memory cleanup.

**Revision:** The instructions should say: *"Reuse `extract_activations()` from `sakana_d2l.py` instead of creating a new `extract_hidden_states` function. If a simplified single-layer extraction is needed, create a thin wrapper that calls `extract_activations` with a single-element `layer_indices` list."* Remove the full function signature from the ARCHITECTURE CHANGE section (lines 103-111) and replace with a reference to the existing function plus any minor adaptation needed.

---

### Issue 6: Key format hard-codes `.self_attn.` prefix

**Evidence:** `hypernetwork.py` lines 180-194: the `forward()` method builds keys as:
```
f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}.lora_A.weight"
```
For `mlp.down_proj`, the correct key would be `layers.{i}.mlp.down_proj.lora_A.weight` (as `sakana_d2l.py` line 417 shows), NOT `layers.{i}.self_attn.mlp.down_proj.lora_A.weight`.

**Revision:** The instructions already note this issue (line 122-123) but do not give a concrete fix. Add explicit guidance: *"Refactor `forward()` to derive the key path from the target module name directly. For modules containing a dot (e.g., `mlp.down_proj`), the full module path replaces the current `.self_attn.{module}` segment. The general pattern should be:*
```python
f"base_model.model.model.layers.{i}.{module}.lora_{ab}.weight"
```
*where `module` is the full dotted path like `self_attn.q_proj` or `mlp.down_proj`."*

This means `target_modules` must change from bare names like `"q_proj"` to full sub-paths like `"self_attn.q_proj"` -- or the key construction logic must map them. The instructions should specify which approach to use.

---

### Issue 7: Weight head interpolation is underspecified

**Evidence:** Instructions lines 68-73 say "use `torch.nn.functional.interpolate` or linear interpolation along the output dimension." `torch.nn.functional.interpolate` operates on spatial/temporal dimensions (designed for images/sequences), not arbitrary linear projection outputs. Applying it to LoRA weight generation head outputs has no theoretical basis and could produce degenerate weights.

**Revision:** If partial weight transfer is kept (see Issue 3), replace the interpolation strategy with one of:
- **Per-layer copying with padding/truncation:** Copy weights for matching layers, zero-init remaining layers. This is well-defined.
- **Random re-initialization of the entire weight_head:** Since the output dimension changes when num_layers or target dims change, the old weight_head is not meaningfully transferable.
- **Remove the interpolation claim entirely** and state that `weight_head` must be retrained from random init whenever the target model architecture changes.

---

### Issue 8: `inject_lora_adapter` cleanup strategy unspecified

**Evidence:** Instructions lines 228-239 say "on exit restore the base model to its original state" without specifying mechanism. PEFT's `get_peft_model` modifies the model in-place.

**Revision:** Specify the cleanup approach explicitly. The recommended pattern is:
```python
@contextmanager
def inject_lora_adapter(base_model, adapter_state_dict, ...):
    from peft import LoraConfig, get_peft_model
    peft_model = get_peft_model(base_model, lora_config)
    peft_model.load_state_dict(adapter_state_dict, strict=False)
    try:
        yield peft_model
    finally:
        # merge_and_unload() returns the unwrapped base model
        peft_model.merge_and_unload()
```
Or alternatively, use PEFT's `disable_adapter_layers()` / `delete_adapter()` if full unloading is too expensive. The instructions should pick one and specify it.

---

### Issue 9: Data format mismatch with existing trajectory format

**Evidence:** Instructions define JSONL as `{"doc", "queries", "answers"}` (line 194). The existing codebase uses trajectory format with `session_id`, `steps`, `generated_code`, `tests_passed` (see `libs/shared/src/shared/rune_models.py`). No migration path is described.

**Revision:** Add a note clarifying that this is a NEW data format specifically for Doc-to-LoRA distillation training, separate from the trajectory format used by the QLoRA training path. The instructions should state: *"This data format is independent of the existing trajectory format. It is designed for knowledge distillation where 'doc' provides context and 'queries'/'answers' define the distillation targets."* Also note that existing trajectory data cannot be directly used without a conversion step.

---

### Issue 10: `sakana_d2l.py` already implements 80% of the requested functionality

**Evidence:** `sakana_d2l.py` already has:
- `download_checkpoint()` -- downloads `qwen_4b_d2l` from HF (line 134)

**Revision:** The instructions should explicitly acknowledge `sakana_d2l.py` and either:

- **Approach B:** Explicitly state why the custom `DocToLoraHypernetwork` is being used instead of Sakana's architecture, and which specific limitations of `sakana_d2l.py` motivate the rewrite.

Currently, the instructions ignore `sakana_d2l.py` entirely, which will lead to confusion and duplicated code.

# Qwen3-Coder-Next Architecture Parameters

## Core Parameters

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **hidden_size** | 2048 |
| **num_hidden_layers** | 48 |
| **num_attention_heads** | 16 (Q), 2 (KV) |
| **num_key_value_heads** | 2 |
| **intermediate_size** | 512 (Expert Intermediate Dimension) |

## Architecture Quirks

### 1. **Hybrid Layout (Major Quirk)**
The model uses a sophisticated **12 × (3 × (Gated DeltaNet → MoE) → 1 × (Gated Attention → MoE))** hybrid architecture:
- **Gated DeltaNet blocks**: 32 linear attention heads for V, 16 for QK (head dimension: 128)
- **Gated Attention blocks**: 16 heads for Q, 2 for KV (head dimension: 256)
- Each pattern repeats 12 times across 48 layers

### 2. **Mixture of Experts (MoE)**
- **Number of Experts**: 512
- **Activated Experts**: 10 (only 3B activated out of 80B total parameters)
- **Shared Experts**: 1
- Enables **3B activated parameters** with **80B total parameters** for efficiency

### 3. **Other Features**
- **Rotary Position Embedding**: 64-dimensional
- **Context Length**: 262,144 tokens natively (256k)
- **No Thinking Mode**: Does not generate `<think></think>` blocks
- **Non-Embedding Parameters**: 79B

This hybrid design combining DeltaNet and Attention layers with sparse MoE routing is the key architectural innovation enabling efficient agentic coding performance.
```

Targeting all experts' down_proj would mean massive adapter sizes (512 experts × per-layer).\n**Fix:** Replace hard-coded `mlp.down_proj` with guidance to identify correct LoRA targets from the model's actual module tree. The frozen/retrained split should reference Sakana's module names (`aggregator.*`, `head.*`), not the custom hypernetwork's (`cross_attend`, `self_attend`).\n\n### Issue 4: Latent dim 512 vs 256\n**Location:** Lines 50, 95, 278\n**Problem:** Instructions say `latent_dim=512`. Custom `DocToLoraHypernetwork` uses `latent_dim=256`.\n**Fix:** Since we're extending Sakana (which uses its own latent_size from `hypernet_config`), this is moot — Sakana's config dictates the dim. Remove hard-coded 512 references; read from checkpoint config.\n\n### Issue 5: Env var name inconsistency\n**Location:** Line 156, shell commands\n**Problem:** `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_CHECKPOINT` vs actual `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH`.\n**Fix:** Use `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH` throughout to match existing code in `training_svc/routers/training.py`.\n\n### Issue 6: `extract_hidden_states` duplicates `extract_activations`\n**Location:** ARCHITECTURE CHANGE lines 103-111\n**Problem:** `sakana_d2l.py` already has `extract_activations()` doing exactly this.\n**Fix:** Reference existing function. The custom hypernetwork remains unchanged for the hash-based token path.\n\n---\n\n## Revised Approach Summary\n\nInstead of modifying `DocToLoraHypernetwork`, the instructions should:\n\n1. **Extend `sakana_d2l.py`** with Qwen3-Coder-Next support\n2. **Create `train_d2l_qwen3.py`** that uses Sakana's HyperLoRA perceiver\n3. **Load `qwen_4b_d2l` checkpoint**, freeze the perceiver, retrain only the output head for Qwen3-Coder-Next's architecture\n4. **Discover LoRA targets dynamically** by inspecting the model's module tree (critical for hybrid DeltaNet+MoE)\n5. **Reuse `extract_activations`** for hidden state extraction\n6. **Keep `DocToLoraHypernetwork` unchanged** — it continues to serve the fast hash-based adapter generation path\n\n---\n\n## Files to modify\n- `instructions/add_lora_training_code.md` — the instruction file being revised in-place\n\n## Verification\nAfter revision, the instructions should be:\n- Consistent with `sakana_d2l.py`'s existing API\n- Aware of Qwen3-Coder-Next's hybrid architecture\n- Using correct env var names (`RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH`)\n- Using `uv run` for all Python commands\n- Not conflating custom DocToLoraHypernetwork with Sakana HyperLoRA\n"

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Revise `instructions/add_lora_training_code.md`

## Context
The instruction document has 17 issues ranging from wrong architecture assumptions to env var naming inconsistencies. The user confirmed:
- **Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next exists** on HuggingFace (verified) — but has a **hybrid DeltaNet + MoE architecture**, not a standard transformer
- **Extend `sakana_d2l.py`** rather than rewriting `DocToLoraHypernetwork`

## Critical Discovery: Qwen3-Coder-Next Architecture

This model is NOT a standard transformer. From the HuggingFace model card:

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| hidden_size | 2048 |
| num_hidden_layers | 48 |
| intermediate_size | 512 (per expert) |
| num_attention_heads | 16 Q / 2 KV |
| num_key_value_heads | 2 |
| **Total params** | **80B** (3B activated via MoE) |
| **Experts** | 512 total, 10 activated, 1 shared |
| **Layout** | 12 × (3 × DeltaNet-MoE + 1 × Attention-MoE) |

**Implications for LoRA targets:**
- Only 12 of 48 layers have standard attention (the rest are DeltaNet linear attention)
- MLP layers are MoE routed — `mlp.down_proj` is per-expert, not a single dense layer
- The instruction's assumption of `mlp.down_proj` as a LoRA target needs rethinking
- Hidden state extraction must handle the hybrid layer types

---

## Issues and Revisions (17 total)

### Issue 1: Architecture assumptions are wrong for hybrid model
**Location:** TARGET MODEL section (lines 17-38), throughout
**Problem:** Instructions assume standard transformer with uniform layers, dense MLP.
**Fix:** Add a WARNING section after the architecture probe. The probe step (step 2) must also check for `config.model_type`, MoE parameters (`num_experts`, `num_activated_experts`), and hybrid layer layout. The instructions should derive LoRA targets dynamically from the config rather than hard-coding `mlp.down_proj`.

### Issue 2: `mlp.down_proj` target is wrong for MoE
**Location:** ARCHITECTURE CHANGE line 121, TRAINING SCRIPT line 175
**Problem:** In MoE models, `mlp.down_proj` is inside expert blocks, not a single dense layer. Targeting all experts' down_proj would mean massive adapter sizes (512 experts × per-layer).
**Fix:** Replace hard-coded `mlp.down_proj` with guidance to identify correct LoRA targets from the model's actual module tree. For hybrid DeltaNet+Attention models, attention projection layers on the attention blocks (12 of 48 layers) are the viable targets. Add: run `model.named_modules()` to discover valid target paths.

### Issue 3: Architecture confusion — custom hypernetwork vs Sakana
**Location:** CHECKPOINT STRATEGY (lines 41-79)
**Problem:** Instructions describe "8 cross-attention blocks (cross_attend in current code)" — but custom `DocToLoraHypernetwork` has 1 `nn.MultiheadAttention`. The `qwen_4b_d2l` checkpoint is a Sakana `HyperLoRA` checkpoint (Idefics2Perceiver), architecturally incompatible with the custom hypernetwork.
**Fix:** Rewrite CHECKPOINT STRATEGY to build on Sakana's `HyperLoRA` architecture via `sakana_d2l.py`. The frozen/retrained split should reference Sakana's module names (`aggregator.*`, `head.*`), not the custom hypernetwork's (`cross_attend`, `self_attend`).

### Issue 4: Latent dim 512 vs 256
**Location:** Lines 50, 95, 278
**Problem:** Instructions say `latent_dim=512`. Custom `DocToLoraHypernetwork` uses `latent_dim=256`.
**Fix:** Since we're extending Sakana (which uses its own latent_size from `hypernet_config`), this is moot — Sakana's config dictates the dim. Remove hard-coded 512 references; read from checkpoint config.

### Issue 5: Env var name inconsistency
**Location:** Line 156, shell commands
**Problem:** `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_CHECKPOINT` vs actual `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH`.
**Fix:** Use `RUNE_HYPERNETWORK_WEIGHTS_PATH` throughout to match existing code in `training_svc/routers/training.py`.

### Issue 6: `extract_hidden_states` duplicates `extract_activations`
**Location:** ARCHITECTURE CHANGE lines 103-111
**Problem:** `sakana_d2l.py` already has `extract_activations()` doing exactly this.
**Fix:** Reference existing function. If a thinner wrapper is needed for single-layer extraction, create one that calls `extract_activations`.

### Issue 7: Key format hard-codes `.self_attn.`
**Location:** ARCHITECTURE CHANGE lines 122-123
**Problem:** `forward()` in `hypernetwork.py` generates keys as `layers.{i}.self_attn.{module}`. For MLP targets or hybrid architectures, the path prefix changes.
**Fix:** Since we're extending sakana_d2l.py (which already handles arbitrary key paths in `_save_sakana_adapter`), this is solved. Reference that implementation.

### Issue 8: Weight head interpolation is underspecified
**Location:** CHECKPOINT STRATEGY lines 68-73
**Problem:** "use torch.nn.functional.interpolate" for LoRA weight generation has no theoretical basis.
**Fix:** Since we're extending Sakana's architecture (not the custom one), the weight_head interpolation strategy changes. For Sakana's HyperLoRA, the `head` module's output dims depend on target model architecture. Specify: re-initialize the head from scratch for mismatched output dims, freeze the perceiver/aggregator, train only the head + any new projection layers.

### Issue 9: `inject_lora_adapter` cleanup unspecified
**Location:** TRAINING SCRIPT lines 228-240
**Problem:** No cleanup mechanism specified.
**Fix:** Specify `peft_model.delete_adapter("default")` or context manager pattern using `peft_model.merge_and_unload()`. The former is cheaper for repeated injection.

### Issue 10: sakana_d2l.py ignored
**Location:** Throughout
**Problem:** The entire document ignores the existing Sakana integration.
**Fix:** Make `sakana_d2l.py` the foundation. The training script should import from it: `load_sakana_checkpoint`, `extract_activations`, `_save_sakana_adapter`.

### Issue 11: `--chunk-size` unexplained
**Location:** Line 181
**Fix:** Define it: "chunk long documents into overlapping windows of this many tokens for hidden state extraction" — or remove if not needed.

### Issue 12: `datasets` noted as optional but is in main deps
**Location:** Implicit in data generator section
**Fix:** No special install note needed. `datasets>=4.6.1` is already in root `pyproject.toml` main dependencies.

### Issue 13: `depth=8` vs actual `depth=4`
**Location:** CONFIG UPDATE line 279
**Fix:** Use `depth=4` to match current codebase, or explicitly note the increase and why.

### Issue 14: Shell commands use `python` instead of `uv run`
**Location:** DELIVERABLES lines 343-369
**Fix:** Replace all `python -m` and `python -c` with `uv run python -m` and `uv run python -c`.

### Issue 15: Test file name references nonexistent convention
**Location:** TESTS line 290
**Fix:** Rename `test_hypernetwork_qwen3.py` to `test_d2l_training.py` (model-agnostic).

### Issue 16: Data format vs trajectory format unacknowledged
**Location:** DATA FORMAT section
**Fix:** Add note clarifying this is a NEW format for D2L distillation, separate from the existing trajectory format used by QLoRA training.

### Issue 17: `generate_adapter()` backward compat path unclear
**Location:** ARCHITECTURE CHANGE lines 113-117
**Fix:** Since we're extending sakana_d2l.py (not modifying DocToLoraHypernetwork), the backward compat concern doesn't apply. The custom hypernetwork remains unchanged for the hash-based token path.

---

## Revised Approach Summary

Instead of modifying `DocToLoraHypernetwork`, the instructions should:

1. **Extend `sakana_d2l.py`** with Qwen3-Coder-Next support
2. **Create `train_d2l_qwen3.py`** that uses Sakana's HyperLoRA perceiver
3. **Load `qwen_4b_d2l` checkpoint**, freeze the perceiver, retrain only the output head for Qwen3-Coder-Next's architecture
4. **Discover LoRA targets dynamically** by inspecting the model's module tree (critical for hybrid DeltaNet+MoE)
5. **Reuse `extract_activations`** for hidden state extraction
6. **Keep `DocToLoraHypernetwork` unchanged** — it continues to serve the fast hash-based adapter generation path

---

- Using `uv run` for all Python commands
- Not conflating custom DocToLoraHypernetwork with Sakana HyperLoRA

    72→      - If the rank dimension is unchanged (rank=8), the A/B matrix
